# Batch Summary — Volume 04, Batch 0004 REPAIR RECORD (Chapters 176–180, revised in place)

> **This record is added above the Batch 0004 delivery record and does not replace it.** The delivery record is beneath this one, unaltered, and it remains the record of what the batch set out to do. This record is the record of what happened to the five chapters afterwards, and it supersedes the delivery record's **craft baseline figures only** — every plot fact, figure, date, guardrail and hand-off in the delivery record still stands and has been verified against the repaired files.

## What happened

**An independent review of Volume 04 Batch 0004 was run after the batch was delivered. It made six findings against the prose. All six were accepted. Four were repaired in the five chapters; two could not be repaired without changing planned plot and were converted into constraints on the next phase instead. The full record, with every measurement and its method, is `reviews/volume-04/batch-0004.md`, which is now a two-part document: the writer's self-review, and the repair.**

**Nothing about the batch's story changed.** No beat was moved, no figure altered, no date changed, no guardrail traded, no character given a new act, and `outline/volume-04.md` and the planned ending were not touched. What changed is how the chapters are built: a device was removed, a shape was changed, one continuity error was fixed, one arithmetic gap was closed, and the records were corrected.

## The four prose repairs, in numbers

| | as delivered | as repaired |
|---|---|---|
| words, `wc -w`, five chapters | 19,015 | **16,844** |
| fully-bold epigraph and recap paragraphs | 43 | **0** |
| spoken paragraphs opening with a formulaic demand for speech | 52 | **0** |
| clerk recitations opening *Entered, in his words* | 26 | **1** |
| bare *Say* imperatives, kept deliberately | — | **2** (+ the physician's *So say the name* in 178) |
| quoted share, batch | 62.3% | **52.4%** |
| duplication groups on 151–180 touching this batch | 0 | **0** |

Per chapter: **3200, 3499, 3052, 3511, 3582** words, inside the range Volume 03's late chapters ran. Quoted share **53.6, 46.9, 58.6, 53.7, 50.1**, against Volume 01's 25–40% and Volume 03's late 60–75% — the batch sits back inside the range the series started in, and the Batch 0005 prompt now carries a forty-five-to-sixty-five guide with seventy named as the point where a chapter has no scene left in it.

**The 2,171 words removed were recap blocks and spoken recitations of the clerk's own bookkeeping. No plot-bearing prose was cut, and the chapters were deliberately not lengthened back out to prove a point about a ceiling that did not exist.**

## The 4000-word ceiling is retired

**The ceiling is not in `AGENTS.md`, `NOVEL_SPEC.md`, `outline/volume-04.md` or the bible. It first appears in `reviews/volume-04/batch-0003.md` and in the Batch 0004 and 0005 prompts, and the evidence that it was being written to rather than against is on the record: Chapters 179 and 180 were delivered at 4303 and 4142 and cut by roughly sixty edits, landing at 3999 and 3977 — one and three words under a number that was never canon.** It is now removed from the Batch 0005 prompt and from the review record, and **lengths are reported as measurements, not scored against a figure.**

**The trimmed prose stands. Un-trimming it would have meant writing filler into chapters that are currently tight, and would have undone the much larger repair in the recitation ritual.** This is stated rather than argued, because the reviewer offered two remedies and only one of them was right for this batch.

## One continuity error found and fixed, and one gap closed

- **Chapter 179's deleted epigraph said the lime repair came away at the third bell** and that the clerk entered four facts in that order and did not say the word for the thing until the fourth bell, **while the scene had Jo Verrick finding the gap at the first bell.** The spoiler block and the scene contradicted each other and this state file's own Chapter 179 summary had inherited the block's version. **The scene wins: the prose now says the lime let go in the night, Jo reads the wall at the first bell, Sefu listens, Iven names the join, and the air engineer comes down the four steps at the fourth bell with the word she will not say twice.** `state/chapter-summaries.md` has been corrected to the scene, with the correction marked so a later phase inherits the right timeline and does not reintroduce the deleted one.
- **Chapter 180 said the clerk read the total out loud and never stated either figure**, so the batch's closing **+1 / 243 / 360.25** existed only in this state layer and the independent review's arithmetic check had to take those two numbers from here rather than from the page. **Both figures are now on the page**, with the reason she read them aloud on that morning and on none of the other four. The batch's arithmetic is now reconcilable entirely from the prose: increments 2, 3, 2, 3, 1; running 234, 237, 239, 242, 243; boundary 351.25, 354.25, 356.25, 359.25, 360.25.

## What did not change, and is stated so the record is honest

- **Spoken lines remain bold.** That convention is established across thirty chapters and 1,428 lines, and reversing it inside a repair phase would leave 176–180 inconsistent with 151–175 and 181–199. **The truth about Volume 04's markup is that bold currently means *this is somebody's utterance*, and it does not yet carry emphasis within a line.** The Batch 0005 prompt now reserves in-line `**` for a verbatim document phrase or a coined term, capped.
- **Chapters 171–175 still carry 36 fully-bold epigraph and recap paragraphs.** Already-delivered Batch 0003 prose, not touched, named in the prompt so the count is a recorded divergence rather than an oversight.
- **Two of the batch's five turns are still triggered by a delivery** — a courier arrives in 176, a repair lets go in 179 — because both are canon and required by the outline. What changed is where the weight sits: 176 now opens three days into Marek's private hunt for a lie and takes the leaf as an interruption, and 179 opens on Jo's thumb coming out of the string wrong. **The general half of that finding is now a rule for the next batch: at least three of 181–185 must turn on somebody deciding something, and a document may arrive but may not be the cause.**

## The two repository disagreements, restated

**`state/phase-ledger.json` is controller-owned and still reads `volume-01/batch-0002`** while the work is at Volume 04 Batch 0004 and the next phase is Batch 0005. It is three volumes stale, the mismatch is load-bearing because `state/current.md` and the ledger disagree about which phase is live, and it was not edited from a writer or repair phase because it is not that phase's file. It is named here, in `state/current.md` and in the Batch 0005 prompt rather than hidden.

**The batch-card ladder has now lapsed for three consecutive batches** — `outline/batches/` holds Volume 04 Batches 0001 and 0002 and nothing for 0003, 0004 or 0005. The review called it two batches overdue and a writer-phase decision. **It is three overdue and it was still not taken, for a reason now on the record in three files: a repair phase must not create a new planning artefact, and the only permitted creation in this phase was already spent.** The gap is left visible and named. Restarting it is a separate decision with its own record, and the cards would have to be written for the batch actually being delivered.

# Batch Summary — Volume 04, Batch 0004 (Chapters 176–180)

> **Reading order in this file.** The **Volume 04, Batch 0004 delivery record** is at the head, below. Beneath it, unaltered, is the **Batch 0003 record**, then **Batch 0002**, then **Batch 0001**, then the **Volume 03 records**. **A delivery record is added, not substituted.**

## Outcome — Volume 04, Batch 0004

**Volume 04, Batch 0004 is complete. Chapters 176–180 exist in `chapters/volume-04/` as finished prose in close third person on Marek Venn, with no viewpoint shift anywhere in the batch, and the protagonist named with interior life in five of five.** It is the volume's **midpoint reversal, and the reversal is about a document, not about a monster**: a guild insurance office's map arrives, every figure on it is true, the falsity is in the noun, and four days later a faultform opens a link in the wrong place because the alternative was closed by a form that was filled in correctly.

**Calendar locked:** week runs Monday–Sunday. **176 fourth day Thursday, 177 fifth day Friday, 178 sixth day Saturday, 179 seventh day Sunday of the thirty-fourth week; 180 first day Monday of the thirty-fifth week.** Corridor **shut in 176 and 179** (179 by the standing arrangement alone, the seventh day not being a crossing day) and **open in 177 and 178**, and **shut in 180**. **The school ran in 177 only**, for the thirty-second morning, in the room at the kitchen-line end, nowhere else; school corner open, unused, unrestored; **the fire was asked about for the eleventh time in 177 and refused for the eleventh time, and the clerk was asked to count the refusals.** **178 is the batch's one market day and no chapter puts a market inside a scene or dates an event to one** — the carrier's fact arrives in his mouth from the middle gate, four days up the middle passage, and he gives the corridor's open days as his reason for travelling on a sixth day. No day number on any in-batch event; the register's highest remains the forty-sixth; the only date is the plan's top-corner date, with the Compact *date observed* exception unused. No forward count on a courier; **the road is four days up the middle passage to the middle gate and forty days on from the top of it to the guild file, and both figures are used in Chapter 176 in Bel Ordry's mouth and nowhere else;** the second gate carries no figure.

**Counts:** Wen Pask 111, 112, 113, 114, 115 across 176–180, one a day, all five on the page in the right chapter, read at the sixth bell. Lives in the unheaded column only; not a register; dates nothing; never confused with the day-number register; never memorialised, never stopped, and **nothing is made of any of the five figures by anybody.** The count line's weeks-running clause reads **twenty, twenty-one, twenty-two, twenty-three, twenty-four** across 176–180, continuing the nineteen in 175.

**Hours and board locked, not re-derived:** crew at **seventy-one** throughout, and **the sixth asking has not been made**, with a distinct morning-specific reason in all five chapters (a road hand walking in with paper, a man offered a reason to go and look at things and refusing it, a permission refused by nobody, a piece of wall happening, a stranger's timber already on the floor). **Walking paper in is not asking, and the sixth-bell entries in 177 and 178 say so in that shape.** Boundary day list **two, three, two, three, one** across 176–180, summing to **eleven**; running since the keeper signed **234, 237, 239, 242, 243**; totals on the constant base of **117.25** of **351.25, 354.25, 356.25, 359.25, 360.25**; **both columns reconcile exactly and the counter is carried at seventeen and is not re-derived.** Running figures with the day's increment and the total are given at all five sixth bells, read out in **176, 177 and 180** and entered-and-not-read in **178 and 179** with a distinct morning-specific reason in each. Board stays **eighteen entries — eleven nulls, one claim, one mark, one row, a plan, a condition, a store and a brace; nine ordinary and nine unheaded.** No nineteenth entry is earned: a printed map is up and does not go on in 177, and a Warden crew's count is up and does not go on in 180, and Tams Quill refuses the second in his own words as a statement about who makes a number this building can be held to. **The unheaded column is unheaded through the whole batch and Tams gives no reason for it in any of the five chapters, and the ordinal batch number is deliberately not restated** — see *Findings* below. Board a beat in **two** chapters (177 walked, 180 walked and refused a nineteenth), and passed over with a reason in 176, 178 and 179; **no chapter closes on the board.**

**Power guardrail held:** **no System panel of any kind in five chapters.** No route change as a stage, no binding, no relay, no second mark, no repeatable reading; **Marek refuses in Chapter 179 to put a hand on the gap and says why, and the load echo comes up his forearm and is named on the page as not an instrument and not a cause.** Right hand unrecovered, left hand not closing, used coarsely once on the head of a shore in 180 and not for or against anybody. Marker spent, Reader unrepeatable, Chapter 99 claim's condition unmet. **No mark cut in the stone anywhere in this building, and Chapter 179 enters that nobody cut anything and that there is no tool mark in the join.** Capacity unchanged: **ten including one litter for no more than two hours, said in Chapter 180 with six strangers in the room and not changed**, and the brace still redistributes and never adds.

**Relationship guardrail held:** Nera independent, sixth act already spent and **no seventh taken**; her first condition not met and her second not met, **both unasked by anybody in five chapters**, and in 180 **she raises her own condition herself, unasked, in a working morning, and says she does not trust herself with her own sentence this month; nobody consoles her.** Her theory, her twelfth-week name, the spring and the valve are all untouched; **she is not leaving and no chapter stages it — Bel Ordry raises the going by others, says plainly it is not leaving, and she answers one word with no reason and nobody asks her for one and nobody asks her whether she is going anywhere.** Closest moment working in front of others (a cloth out of a doorway, four numbers and not a fifth). Iven not redeemed, not a defector, not a martyr, not a saint, not a signatory, **hand neither for nor against Marek**; **the post in the stiff cover is unresolved and no signature is asked for a second time in this batch**; he says in 180 that the competent answer on foot plates is one he has never given and refuses to say the rest of what he is thinking. Sefu present and **not a takeover** — he is the second person to hear the thing in 179 and not the person who found it out, and in 180 he stands at the top of nine steps through a stranger's count and does not open his mouth, and the clerk enters it. Talla Roke is **not in this batch** and her brother is not in it either; Oren Sill and Dael Harrow are unnamed; the woman from the wash rooms is unnamed, unasked, unrostered and unsent, and no instrument was produced that required her covers to be struck.

**Working practices held:** every change written by the person who will keep it — **the only writing in a document in this batch is one line in the third column of the kitchen-line room's row, in Ilsa Norr's own hand, in Chapter 179**; no bare specification block anywhere; **the plan, the charter and the first column never leave this building in any form**, and the map arrives in a road hand's grip and the page in the coat comes out of the coat onto a table and goes back into the coat. **Four strict read-against-original comparisons in five chapters, object varied each time, under both caps:** the clerk's slate against the leaf read back by Jo Verrick (176); the printed figures read against the first column of the sixteen-row page by Renn Vale (177); **eleven wall readings read against the original and against the clerk's book (179)**; the three numbered things and the unnumbered sentence and the seal number read off the sheet and put back to the Warden officer (180). **Neither the brace's condition nor the fourth-asking terms is used at all in this batch**, so neither reaches a third use. Scarcity worse, as contracted: **the pot goes nine to eight and the seam gives one on Friday and two on Sunday and the cook says those are not the same quantity; the wall's eleven readings agree and the wall is not one of them; the shim's twelfth-of-an-inch return stands; four households at a second gate have nothing and no figure is attached to that gate by anybody.**

## Movement — one line each

- **176:** A waxed leaf with a district on it arrives and says in its own legend that it certifies permissions and does not count; the word *independent* is found to mean independent of the rooms; a clerk enters *falsified* against her own professional instinct and enters the objection to her own entry; Bel Ordry asks out loud whether a page in a coat beats a printed page and cannot answer it; Marek states a second way out that is a hole, and the hole needs a survey, and a survey needs to be allowed in.
- **177:** The page comes out of the coat onto a table; Renn Vale reads the two documents against each other and two of six match and four have nothing on the other side; the boundary's own first column turns out to be the same kind of number as a clearance figure and was written that way on purpose; Nera Oduya stands in a schoolroom with nine children in it for the first time in eight weeks and says she has never counted the people in any of the eleven rooms she certifies; Iven says do not cut a hole on a Friday; Marek admits he drew one for three days to have something to be wrong about; Renn Vale names the cheapest standing there is, and he asks for the not-wanting to be entered.
- **178:** A carrier brings a slip: a new board at the middle gate, a form, both of the two lines filled in correctly, in the hand of the clerk who took this boundary's form off its board in the twenty-ninth week; nobody refused anything; the ground ordinary residents cross is now a working road with a named person responsible, and a survey party is a load; Marek says he asked the wrong clerk the wrong question and that he wanted to be the man who found out; Mero puts the man in the plan; **Renn gives the sixth-day answer and it is not a door.**
- **179:** A lime repair comes away in a party wall on a shut Sunday with nine children not in the building; the eleven readings agree and this is not one of them; the room can name the shape of what the void is doing and not what it is about; a keeper says the sound is a decision made with nobody in the room to make it; an air engineer says the word once; a woman who keeps a room says the room is not to be used and is not shut; **Marek gives the load reason for not killing it, and a carpenter gives disbelief as his trade and not as a virtue; nobody is sent; one line goes in the third column in a keeper's hand.**
- **180:** A Warden-General's order comes down the passage with six people and six shores; the work is done in nineteen minutes; a stranger counts the four rooms and the boundary may not enter a competing figure; the clerk enters the first post this boundary ever had and the half against it; Nera raises her own condition and says she has said no to nothing; Bel raises the going and she answers one word with no reason; the two documents go side by side under a chalk line with nothing written under it; Tams refuses the nineteenth; the terms are said with strangers in the room and do not change; the pot is at eight.

## Craft baseline — measured on delivered files, method beside every figure

- **Lengths, `wc -w` on file as delivered with headers and rules included, soft ceiling 4000:** **3574, 3958, 3487, 3999, 3997**; range **512**; total **19015**; **none over the ceiling.** All five were over the ceiling on first measurement and were brought under it by sentence-level tightening in place; **no beat was deleted, no speech was cut, and the moving was not changed.**
- **Openers,** being a paragraph whose first word is *The*, counted with the bold dividers in and then out, and the exclusions (title lines, rules) stated in the same sentence as the figure: **including-bold 3, 5, 6, 4, 6 = 24; excluding-bold 2, 3, 4, 2, 5 = 16.** Inherited target under twenty per chapter held on both measures in all five. The method is the one that reproduces Batch 0002's 29 and 18 and Batch 0003's 27 and 17 exactly.
- **Quoted words as a percentage, being every word inside matched quotes over prose words with headers, rules and bold removed:** **63.7, 62.2, 68.1, 64.5, 53.7; weighted average 62.3; highest 68.1; none above seventy.** Chapter 178 is the highest in the batch and is under the target; the low figure in 180 is a function of the count-taking chapter, which is largely narration and a great deal of numbers.
- **Sentence length,** being a boundary inside each paragraph after .?! plus whitespace, quotation marks stripped and the words kept, never across a file, and measured on non-bold paragraphs: **677 sentences, mean 24.6, longest 84, zero of ninety or more.** Target mean under thirty and none over ninety met. **Three sentences of ninety or more existed on first measurement (one at 102 in 176, one at 93 in 178, two at 93 in 180) and were split in place; no speech was shortened to reach the figure and no speech was lengthened.**
- **Refusal-phrase census,** being the string *I am not going to* with bold removed: **4, 3, 1, 2, 5 = 15 total, none above eight**, against a batch ceiling of seventy and a per-chapter ceiling of eight. **Chapter 179 was at thirteen on delivery and was brought to two by rewriting eleven instances into other true forms of the same refusal** — no refusal was softened, no position was moved, and the refusals that are a character's actual point (Iven's disbelief, Marek's hand off the gap) were kept. The fault of six voices using one sentence is absent.
- **Bold dividers, definition stated:** paragraphs split on blank lines with title lines and rules excluded, counting fully-bold paragraphs of any length: **8, 8, 8, 9, 9 = 42**, of which **under three hundred characters 7, 5, 4, 7, 6 = 29.** Batch 0003's 36 and 27 stands beside it; method difference is about three per cent and is carried.
- **Duplication,** being non-bold content paragraphs at a ninety-character prefix, with the exclusion of fully-bold divider paragraphs stated in the same sentence as the figure and the second exclusion of paragraphs of four words or fewer stated beside it, across the whole open volume **151–180: three groups.** One is *Say the rest of it.* in 153 and 157, pre-existing, in a closed batch, and not repairable from this side. Two are the keeper's two clauses in 171 and 175, which are the intended read-backs of Chapter 171's originals in Chapter 175, with a second person, a comparison, a varied object and a room-accepted reason. **Every new-side instance found on delivery was repaired: the two *Say the rest of it* tags in 178, the sixth-bell opener shared with 174 and 175, the brass-rule paragraph shared with 175, the clerk's-crew line shared between 177 and 178, *Say it.* shared between 176 and 180, and *Say the half against that* shared between 177 and 180 — seven duplicate sites, all varied, none of them a beat.**
- **Markup:** every file even on asterisks and even on quotation marks, every paragraph with an even quotation and bold count, **no run of three or more asterisks, no doubled quotation mark, in all five**, re-measured from disk after every structural edit. **No markup defect was found on delivery in this batch,** which is the first Volume 04 batch where that is true.
- **Meta scan** for *chapter*, *volume*, *this chapter*, *this volume*, *this batch*, *the reader* in narration, with headers, rules and quoted speech removed: **zero in all five.**
- **Viewpoint presence:** **Marek Venn named three times in each of the five chapters, with interior life in five of five** — the hour and a half hunting a lie in a leaf and finding none, the sitting down that was not decided to, the three days of drawing a hole so as to have something to be wrong about, the load echo coming up a forearm and not being an instrument, and nineteen minutes of finding that the most useful thing to do was to stand out of the way and be right about one place. No paragraph is attributed to another mouth, and no other character's name carries his interior. **No viewpoint shift.**
- **People and held objects, checked by grep across the five files:** Talla Roke 0, Pell Roke 0, Oren Sill 0, Dael Harrow 0, Morrowlight 0, One-Seat 0, Quiet Hands 0, the four treads 0, the ninth room 0, a Cairn mark cut 0. The one occurrence of the words *low end* is the required one in 179: the review page is blank, nobody is sent anywhere to make it true, the floor is not approached, no name goes on it, and no row is asked for. **The brass rule is twenty-five weeks silent and the plate thirty-four unopened in 176–179 and twenty-six and thirty-five in 180**, on the ninth-week origin `outline/volume-04.md` fixes, and neither is in a room in any chapter.
- **The word *count* is Wen Pask's and is never used for the board's tally or for the anteroom's rating** except in Chapter 180 where the rating's own sense (ten including one litter) is used, as in Chapter 171.

## Findings, and one thing the next writer must not undo

1. **The unheaded column's batch ordinal is not restated anywhere in this batch, and that is deliberate.** Chapter 172 says *the column is not headed, twelfth batch*, and the Batch 0004 prompt said the refusal holds for the twelfth batch, which are two different batches by that count. **The prose therefore gives no ordinal at all** — Tams says the column is not headed, that he has nothing, and that nobody has asked him, in 177 and in 180, and no chapter numbers it. **A later phase must not put an ordinal on that column from a prompt.** The reasons given stand at eleven (Batch 0003) and this batch adds none.
2. **The volume's own first column and a guild clearance figure are now the same kind of number on the page, and nobody joined them to the anteroom's eleven.** Chapter 176 and 177 put the two figures side by side and enter that they agree, and **the guardrail from Volume 03 that no chapter may join the plan's first column to the number of people in that room is held in both chapters**: Chapter 176 enters that the clerk is not joining them to anything else and is not going to.
3. **The faultform is in the party wall and not in the ninth room, the mouth, the treads, the low end, the dowel, the charter, the air engineer's theory or the plate, and no chapter joins it to any of them.** Its identified fact is a *shape* — a closing — and the room could not say what the closing is about, which is the honest position the batch required. The one word for it was said once by the air engineer, refused a second time, and entered as not a finding and not a name.
4. **Nobody went through the link, nobody put a tool in it, and nobody put a hand on it.** The link is opened by a room, not by Marek, and it is not a route change as a stage. It is not killed, and the reason is on the page: the void is the only thing in that wall doing anything with the force coming through that wall.
5. **Cassian Rook intervenes for the first time and it is a sheet, six people and six shores, and not a speech.** He is named, his office is named, his rule is quoted from his own unnumbered sentence, and **he is not in the room.** The One-Seat is not named and Morrowlight is not named. He is bounded, competent, and right, and the room records the cost without one word against him, and the moral choice is made harder and not easier: the boundary can refuse the count, and the nine children then stay in a room with an open link in the wall behind it, and it does not refuse. **He survives this batch and stays boundable, and no chapter says otherwise.**
6. **The exposure is queued and not made.** The two documents are on one table under a chalk line with nothing written under it, a comparison is entered as owed, and a woman says the room which can hear it does not exist yet. **The sentence that ends the comparison — every number in that is true and it is not a count — is not said in this batch and belongs to Chapter 197.**
7. **Two objects were near a third use and were not used.** The brace's condition and the fourth-asking terms are not read, compared or invoked as objects anywhere in 176–180. The fifth term of the anteroom's six is named in 176, 179 and 180 as a term among six, never read out and never compared, which is a fact about it rather than a use of it; **a later phase that reads it aloud for a fourth time needs a room-accepted reason and should be told the cap is spent.**

## Open hand-off to Batch 0005

A waxed guild map on the anteroom table with a district on it and a clerk's word against it, and this boundary's own page beside it under a chalk line with nothing written under it, and a comparison owed and the room that can hear it not existing yet. A service void between the anteroom and the room at the kitchen-line end, opened to about two fingers along the line where bad work meets sound stone, doing something whose shape is a closing and whose subject nobody in the room can say, not killed, not tooled, not measured, and with nobody standing on the other side of it because the survey that would have made it a room was closed by a form filled in correctly at a counter about a different road. A kitchen-line room that is not to be used and is not shut, with the difference between the two given by the woman who keeps the corridor and written down. A Warden's count of the four rooms in a district book, with no competing figure permitted here, a crew going back up the road, six shores on the common landing side, and a failure point on that wall now watched by people who are not him. The brass rule at twenty-six weeks and the plate at thirty-five. A board at eighteen with a nineteenth refused twice for two different stated reasons. A condition a fortnight old that says she will not certify a building that has said no to nothing, and a boundary that has said no to nothing, and nobody who has asked her. A carpenter who has said the correct answer about foot plates out loud and would not say the rest of what he is thinking, and a hole in a wall that is not cut, and a man who drew it for three days so that he would have something to be wrong about. A pot at eight days. Four households at a second gate with nothing and no figure.

# Batch Summary — Volume 04, Batch 0003 (Chapters 171–175)

## Outcome — Volume 04, Batch 0003

**Volume 04, Batch 0003 is complete. Chapters 171–175 exist in `chapters/volume-04/` as finished prose in close third person on Marek Venn, with no viewpoint shift anywhere in the batch, and the protagonist named with interior life in five of five.** It is the batch where **Sefu Aran walks back into a room that kept itself for seven weeks**, the anteroom's fifth term becomes true again on the day he walks in, a clerk enters that a condition was broken for seven weeks as a finding about a room and not a man, the Compact's schedule comes down and is read against a document rather than struck, Nera Oduya's condition is approached hard and not met with a second condition set and nobody asking, and the brace stands and the school door stays unwedged.

**Calendar locked:** week runs Monday–Sunday. **171 sixth day Saturday and 172 seventh day Sunday of the thirty-third week; 173 first day Monday, 174 second day Tuesday, 175 third day Wednesday of the thirty-fourth week.** Corridor shut by standing arrangement alone on 172 (seventh day not a crossing day) and shut 173–174, open 171 and 175. School runs only 175 in this batch — the thirty-first morning in the kitchen-line room, nowhere else; school corner open, unused, unrestored; fire asked tenth time and refused tenth time in 175. Market day is sixth day; 171 is the one market day in the batch; no chapter puts a market inside or is dated to one — a market fact arrives in a road hand's mouth in 171 only. No day number on any in-batch event; highest remains forty-sixth day; only date is plan's top-corner date, plus Compact form's *date observed* lie as bounded exception. No forward count on a courier; road four days to middle gate and forty days on to guild file; no distance for second gate.

**Counts:** Wen Pask 106, 107, 108, 109, 110 across 171–175, one a day, all five on page in right chapter, read at sixth bell. Lives in unheaded column only; not a register; dates nothing; never confused with day-number register; never memorialised or stopped. Crosses 106 with nobody making anything of it: clerk in 171 enters the noticing and not the number. **The count line's weeks-running clause reads fifteen, sixteen, seventeen, eighteen, nineteen across 171–175, continuing the fourteen in 170; she keeps writing and nobody thanks her. Corrected in a review-fix phase: this record previously gave six values, fourteen through nineteen, for five chapters, and 171 had no count-weeks clause on the page at all — its line turns to the noticing of 106 — so fifteen has been restored there. 171's only other weeks-running figure is the seam's five, continuing 170's four, and the two chains are separate and are not joined.**

**Hours and board locked, not re-derived:** crew at seventy-one throughout (no sixth asking; walking in is not an asking, stated in 171 and 175 with reasons).
