# Batch Summary — Volume 04, Batch 0002 (Chapters 161–170)

> **Reading order in this file.** The **Volume 04, Batch 0002 delivery record** is at the head, below. Beneath it, and unaltered, is the **Volume 04, Batch 0001 record**, then the **Volume 03, Batch 0005 record** (the volume's climax and its open handoff), then the Batch 0004, 0003, 0002, 0001 and Volume 03 Planning records. **A delivery record is added, not substituted.**

## Outcome

**Volume 04, Batch 0002 is complete. Chapters 161–170 exist in `chapters/volume-04/` as finished prose in close third person on Marek Venn, with no viewpoint shift anywhere in the batch, and the protagonist named with interior life in ten of ten.** It is the batch that carries **the Bracer stage**, and `bible/power-system.md` is the authority it was written against, as `outline/volume-04.md` requires.

**The batch's spine, as delivered:** a carpenter lays out on an anteroom table that a joint is not a pin, that a joint with no pin in it has to be a shape that stands up on its own, and that a shape that stands up on its own can be taken apart by a person with a knife and nothing else; **the four things this building does not have are iron, bronze, a kiln and a second pair of hands, and the carpenter puts the one about people last and says he has never once put it last before**; the brace is joined to a mark by **cutting the wood to the stone and not the stone to the wood, so that no mark is cut anywhere in this building, and the joint is a beech shim a finger wide in a hollow worn in nine-hundred-year-old stone, and the carpenter says its edges will crush in about two years and that the person who will know is a term in a clerk's book**; **time is the second of the four sentences and it is the one this building cannot beg for, and the time is four days in seven, and two of the four are the first week of this batch**; **a clerk asked the crew for a fifth time, in a room, with the terms out loud, Renn Vale named as the person who can be told no, and the three who could not be asked named with a fourth set of reasons, and the hours moved by four and a half and the figure is seventy-one**; **every person whose hands are on the brace says out loud what they are holding and what stops it, and the fourth of the four is a man who keeps a page and what stops it is the word *door***; **two people said no to holding it — a man who reads the board, whose reason is a page that has been blank for thirty-two weeks because a person who is willing is not a person who is standing there, and a woman who keeps a corridor, whose reason is that a person who can take a thing out cannot be one of the hands that put it in — and nobody forced anybody, and the price of not forcing them was a day of delay and a name in a clerk's book**; **a cook said yes and said the half against it first, that she is the woman who would not put a dead man's share into that pot for a brace and is now going to hold a piece of wood for one**; **a carpenter was asked to sign the record of the thing he built and said no, and gave the reason that his name is in the second line of a stiff cover four and a half days up a road and he has not decided about that one**; **a man who keeps a page said out loud that he is suspicious of the man with the store, and gave the half of the suspicion that argues against it, and said a clerk arranged the timing and that it was a dirty thing to have arranged**; **the air engineer asked for a second work, was wrong in public before anybody could be kind to her about it, and set a limit with no number and no day on it**; **a physician named the brace's failure point *the place where it comes out* before anything was stood under it**; **the condition was met and admitted in a room, out loud, by the two people it names, with a physician's written refusal to certify standing next to it and three things said by the woman who keeps the room in the space where a reason goes**; **a room's rating is a word said to a page, and the word is *door*, and a brace is going across the head of the door the word is about, and nobody has checked whether the word is now about the same thing, and the word was said twice anyway and the clerk repeated it**; **the brace went in on the third day of the thirty-third week with nine children taught on the other side of the wall, and the frame came into true by a twelfth of an inch, and the anteroom's count is ten including one litter and did not change**; **it came out on the fourth day, in eleven minutes, because the woman who keeps the room knelt at the ninth hearth stone at the second bell and found its bed about a third of an inch higher than it was on Friday morning, and said the three clauses of a condition she had written a fortnight ago, and nobody argued with her and nobody was sent to look**; **a physician would not put his own fingers in the gap because he has spent eleven days asking this room to read a wall and not themselves, and a woman on a landing refused to ask the man who built it and asked the woman it could hurt instead**; **it went back in a foot and a half lower, where the wall is stone and not the repair, and a fifth sound wedge went under the ninth hearth stone's bed, and a clerk read four terms this building wrote for itself three weeks ago against the original and compared them, and they were the same, and they bound a work and not a building**; **the count is at one hundred and five; the pot is at nine days; a board has eighteen entries and nine of them are under a column with nothing over the top of it; and a man who reads that board gave the tenth reason for not heading it and then, a week later, said he had nothing and manufactured nothing.**

**Three things the batch refused, deliberately. No System panel of any kind** — no stat block, no item table, no quest text, no experience bar, no rank, and the correct amount of System in this batch is none. **No power stage beyond the Bracer** — the brace is built, it stands, it is watched, and it does not increase the anteroom's capacity; the Marker is spent and its mark is still off the wall, the Reader reading is not repeatable, the Chapter 99 claim's condition is unmet, the right hand is lost and the left hand does not close. **And no mark cut in the stone anywhere in this building** — the four marks and the groove on the lintel over the school corner's mouth are joined to the brace by fitting wood to stone, and the clerk enters that fact four times, once of them deliberately beside the ninth striking-out of a schedule, and will not say why.

## The calendar this batch is locked to

**The week runs Monday to Sunday. 161–165 the thirty-second week; 166–170 the thirty-third.** 161 the first day, a Monday; 162 the second, a Tuesday; 163 the third, a Wednesday; 164 the fourth, a Thursday; 165 the fifth, a Friday; 166 the first day of the thirty-third week, a Monday; 167 the second, a Tuesday; 168 the third, a Wednesday; 169 the fourth, a Thursday; 170 the fifth, a Friday. **The corridor is shut on the first, second and fourth days and open on the third, fifth and sixth, and the seventh day is not a crossing day; the school runs on the three open days.** So the corridor is **shut in 161, 162, 164, 166, 167 and 169** and **open in 163, 165, 168 and 170**, and the **school ran in 163, 165, 168 and 170 — the twenty-seventh, twenty-eighth, twenty-ninth and thirtieth mornings — all four in the room at the kitchen-line end.** The school corner is open and is not used and no chapter in this batch put the school back in it. **No school day was restored and no closure was removed.** The market day is the sixth day of a week and is not in this batch. **The spoken measure of elapsed weeks is a separate register**, is not re-derived, is not corrected and dates nothing; from the thirtieth week onward *thirty* is the week clock itself. Counts that track the week clock are re-derivable and monotonic in this batch: **the review page at the low end has been blank for thirty-two weeks in the thirty-second week and thirty-three in the thirty-third**; **the plan has been transcribing the anteroom's count for the same span**; **the clerk has been this boundary's clerk for the same span**; and the anteroom frame's packing went in on the third day of the eighteenth week. **No day number is attached to any in-batch event; the day-number register's highest value remains the forty-sixth day; the only date on any object is the date in the top corner of the plan.** **Wen Pask's count is 96, 97, 98, 99, 100, 101, 102, 103, 104 and 105 across the ten chapters, one a day, all ten on the page in the right chapter**, and it is not a register, it dates nothing, and the two may never be confused or merged. **The brass survey rule has said nothing for twenty-three weeks in the thirty-second week and twenty-four in the thirty-third, on the ninth-week origin that `outline/volume-04.md` fixes and says it fixes, and the wrapped plate on the same shelf is thirty-two and thirty-three and is the consistent register.** **No forward count of days or weeks on a courier**: the road is four days up the middle passage to the middle gate and forty days on from the top of it to a guild file, no figure is attached to the second gate, and a store four days up that passage is four days up it and no other figure is attached to it.

## Movement

- **Chapter 161 — What A Joint Is. Thirty-second week, first day, a Monday, the corridor shut, the school did not run.** Iven Tallo clears a space on the anteroom table with the back of his left hand and lays two pieces of beech across each other and says **a joint is not a pin, a joint is two pieces agreeing to stay in the same place**, and gives four ways of making them agree and then says the one the room wants: **a joint with no pin in it has to be a shape that stands up on its own, and a shape that stands up on its own can be taken apart by a person with a knife and nothing else, and that is the reason he wants to build it this way and he is not going to be asked for it twice.** The man who keeps the page says the half against from the step: a joint anybody can undo. **Four things this building does not have: iron, bronze, a kiln, and a second pair of hands, and the carpenter puts the one about people last and says he has been here thirty-two weeks and has never once put the people last.** The spent nine-metre line is the cord, and Marek Venn says what a spent line is — it does not snap, it goes slack and keeps going slack, and it tells you first — and that a spent line in a joint is a joint that will come apart in a bad month and he would rather it came apart in a bad month than a good one. **The condition is read out by Renn Vale and compared and is the same, and the schoolteacher says she does not want it read back to her twice a week to see whether the words have moved, because the words are the easy part.** Time is the second of the four sentences and Jo Verrick gives it: **four days in seven, and two of the four are this week.** Bel Ordry notices out loud that the air engineer's standing is the first thing in thirty-two weeks that has put itself in a column without anybody's permission, says the half against, and says he is not going to write the line. **Marek says he had a standing for a week and did not notice it and has been proud of it, and the clerk enters the want and says it is the most ordinary thing in this book and does not count for anything.** Board walked at seventeen. Count ninety-six.
- **Chapter 162 — The Fifth Asking. Second day, a Tuesday, the corridor shut.** **The fifth asking in thirty-three weeks**, in a room, with the terms out loud, Renn Vale named as the person who can be told no, **and the three who could not be asked named with a fourth set of reasons and two of the three are not the names she used last week**: Leena Orr, because she is the only person whose whole trade is reading a thing that is already built and this is a thing that is not built yet; Jo Verrick, because she reads that wall at a first bell and the day the brace goes in is the day the wall she reads has a push coming out of it; **and Pell Roke, because the work is holding, and a boy of fourteen on a crutch is not going to be one of the four that hold, and the clerk is not going to be the one who asks him to watch instead.** Tams Quill is asked and asks for his hours to be named before somebody names them, and takes half an hour. **Four and a half hours: two of Iven Tallo's, two of Marek Venn's, half of Tams Quill's. The crew's column moves to seventy-one.** The terms include the four sentences of the fourth asking, repeated word for word, **and Renn Vale makes the clerk read them again and asks which of them she is standing behind, because a brace is coming and two of those four are about a brace.** Bel Ordry finds the contradiction — the terms say the five take no wedge and no bar, and there are two sound wedges on a bench — and Renn Vale says **that is not a contradiction, that is a door: a boundary that is not one of the five can do what it likes with its own wedges and can write terms for five other people that forbid it, and then do the thing itself.** The clerk adds a sixth sentence: **a work that stops is a work that was stopped in a room by a person who said so.** Marek asks for it to be made into a term and then says he did not want to. Figures entered and not read. Count ninety-seven.
- **Chapter 163 — The Shape Four Hands Hold. Third day, a Wednesday, the corridor open, the school ran for the twenty-seventh morning.** The fire in that room has now not been lit for eleven weeks and the cook is asked for the seventh time and refuses a seventh time. **The six cut pieces are not long enough and the answer is not more wood: the head is cut on a splay, which is longer and wider, and the carpenter says the wide face is carrying four tons and that is arithmetic and not a compliment to the hand that cut it.** Four people hold the shape and one person cuts. **Tams Quill says no, and gives the reason: the page at the low end says somebody stands there and has been blank for thirty-two weeks, and it is blank because a person who is willing is not a person who is standing there, and he asks for that to be entered before anybody is kind to him about it, and says he would be good at it.** Dalla Sen says nobody is going to be made to hold it, by her or by a term or by a pot at nine. **Talla Roke asks what a person in this building is supposed to do when it lets go, and gives the answer: it will have to be a person who is not the man who built it.** A child of nine asks a carpenter two questions about wood and is told a rule about safety by a man who keeps nothing. Count ninety-eight.
- **Chapter 164 — Cut To The Stone. Fourth day, a Thursday, the corridor shut, the school did not run.** **The survey's four sentences are read out, compared against the book, and the room says which of the four things it still has**, and the fourth of them is the mark and the man who cut it cannot read it. **A carpenter says a brace is joined to a mark by cutting the wood to the stone and not the stone to the wood, and says he will take a tool out of anybody's hand who goes up there with it, and the answer to the hollow worn in the bed is a beech shim a finger wide, and the shim carries load on its edges and will crush in about two years, and the person who will know is a term in a clerk's book, and a term in a clerk's book is a person who has not been born yet.** **Nera Oduya, from the third step, says the school corner's mouth is the only opening in this building that goes from the corridor into a room nine children are taught in, that it is uncertified, that it is the only one she can open without asking anybody's permission, and that both of those are on the same lintel, and that the air is going to be asked for as a work.** **Tams Quill gives the tenth reason for refusing to head the column, and it is not about him and it is not the ninth: I do not know what this building is for, and I am not going to print a blank where the answer goes, because a blank under a heading is a hole with a lid on it and a man in a bad month is going to fall into it.** Board walked. Count ninety-nine.
- **Chapter 165 — The Fourth Hand. Fifth day, a Friday, the corridor open, the school ran for the twenty-eighth morning.** The cook asks for the fire an eighth time and refuses, and then does the pot: **five people working for two days on a thing nobody asked them to be paid for in a pot means a pot at nine is going to be eight, and the only reason it is not eight today is a seam that gave two jars instead of one, and she is not going to say a brace is being paid for in that seam and the clerk enters the two next to each other and does not write the word brace beside either.** **Renn Vale asks to be asked and says no, and the reason is that she is one of the two people who can take the thing out, and a person who can take a thing out cannot be one of the hands that put it in, and a room will look for the person whose hand was on it, and if that person is the person allowed to take it out then the room has learned the wrong lesson and it will have learned it from her.** Mero Senn says the rule and Dalla Sen enters his sentence directly after hers. **Mikka Hale says yes and says the half against it first, and asks a room to notice that the sentence about not putting a dead man's share in the pot and the sentence about holding a piece of wood for a brace are about the same thing.** **Iven Tallo is asked to sign the record of the thing he built and says no: he has got his name in the second line of a stiff cover four and a half days up a road and has not decided about that one, and he is not going to put a name in the second line of a second thing until he has.** **The shim is cut and not put in the heap, and the school door is looked at and has no wedge in it, and the answer is a person, and the person is one, and the page where a person would go is blank and is not going to be used.** Count one hundred, announced on Thursday so that nobody is surprised, and nobody was.
- **Chapter 166 — The Air On The Other Side. Thirty-third week, first day, a Monday, the corridor shut.** **A man of forty-nine went up the middle passage at the first bell with three of his own and the fir from the store, and the beech stayed, and he asked for nothing on his way out.** **Bel Ordry says out loud that he is suspicious of the man with the store, and gives the reason — forty-one pieces of beech, eleven years, nothing here can pay him for it and nothing here can be found by it, and a keeper who has been given a favour by a place he is not in has less room to say no the second time — and says the half that argues against it, and says a clerk arranged the timing and that it was a dirty thing to have arranged and he would have said it with the man standing there.** **Nera Oduya asks for a second work in the practice, names the three she is not asking with the reason for each, and the work is: does the brace change the air, and if it does what is the limit, with no number and no day on it.** **Mero Senn asks for the failure point to be named out loud before a person is stood under it, and it is named *the place where it comes out*, and it is a phrase and not a term, and it is not the ninth hearth stone's bed because he has not looked at it, and it is not on a stone.** Jo Verrick reads it back and compares. **Pell Roke reads nine figures off a slate, having been told what they are first: eight readings of a quarter of an inch and two nothings, and the two nothings are after a Wednesday when the school ran.** **Nera Oduya is wrong in public and says so herself first: a frame that has been moving and a frame that has been held do not leak the same, and the corridor is the only thing that moves air between the anteroom and that schoolroom when the mouth is shut, and she did not put those four things together in a month of looking at that end of the building.** **And the half against her own answer: the wall stopped on a Wednesday and a Wednesday is a day nine children are in there, and she is not going to stand in a room and say nine children are not a load because she would like the wall to have stopped for a reason she can live with.** She sets a limit and says she is not going to be one of the two women who wrote the condition on the brace, and says the hard part, which is that being the only person who may turn that wheel is what an authority is and there is no part of it anybody should find comfortable. Count one hundred and one.
- **Chapter 167 — The Condition Is Met. Second day, a Tuesday, the corridor shut, the school did not run and lost nothing.** **Mero Senn reads the wall nine times and it read nothing twice and he says he has a shape and not a conclusion, and a wall can stop for a week or stop for good and there is no instrument in this building that tells those apart.** The condition is read out by a second person and compared and is the same. **Bel Ordry raises the thing nobody had checked: a word can change what it is about, and the anteroom's word is *door*, and a brace is going across the head of the door the word is about, and in six weeks a room that says the word is going to hear the brace in it and not only the door, and nobody has asked whether those are the same sound.** He asks for it as an objection and not as a discovery. **The objection is unanswered and the word is said twice anyway and the clerk repeats it and the count is ten including one litter.** **Mero Senn puts his refusal to certify on the table because nobody asked him to, and says the refusal has not changed and the reason for it has, because a fortnight ago he could point at a thing and say *this*, and today there is a thing on the other side of the wall and he cannot point at the thing it will hurt until somebody stands in the room and looks.** **The two people still agree that the wall can take it, with the refusal standing next to it, and the woman who keeps the room says three things in the space where a reason goes: that she has never liked the condition and is not pretending otherwise, that nine children will think she is frightened and they will be right, and that she is glad the wall read nothing and is saying it out loud because the condition is nearly free and this is the one thing she can make it cost.** The condition is met. **And a man with a figure in a private book stands in a room and does not say the figure, and the clerk enters that the two people talking about a wall were a physician and a schoolteacher, and that this is a change and not a loss, and he says nothing about it at all.** Count one hundred and two. Board walked at seventeen.
- **Chapter 168 — The Frame Comes Into True. Third day, a Wednesday, the corridor open, the school ran for the twenty-ninth morning.** The cook asks for the fire a ninth time. **The agreement is said out loud and four people say what they are holding and what stops it, and the fourth of the four is a man with a page in his coat and what stops it is the word, and the half against it is that a word is not a wall and that this morning he discovered the thing he has been protecting is the word and not the wood.** The brace goes up in about forty seconds and nobody speaks during them. **Jo Verrick takes a figure off the frame before she says anything about it: a twelfth of an inch back, the first better number anybody in this building has had off that frame in thirty-three weeks, and she says what it means and what it does not mean and she says the second one louder, to the cook, because the cook asked for the anteroom's number out loud in a room on Monday.** **Ten including one litter, unchanged, and a brace that makes a room safe by making it hold more people is the failure.** **The anteroom's terms are said, the word is said, and the clerk repeats it, and Bel Ordry says which door, and Renn Vale asks which one, and he says the outer service door, the one a body goes out of, and a piece of beech over its head has not changed what it is for.** Nine children come out the ordinary way past the anteroom, and the last one stops in the doorway and looks up at the brace and goes on down the nine steps. Count one hundred and three.
- **Chapter 169 — The Bed Under The Ninth Stone. Fourth day, a Thursday, the corridor shut, the school did not run.** **Nobody was sent to look. The woman who keeps the room went in on her own at the second bell, on a shut day, because that is what she does, and knelt at the ninth hearth stone for longer than the other eight took together, and the bed is about a third of an inch higher than it was on Friday morning and the stone has not moved and the flags have not moved and the board is where it was.** **She says she wants the brace out, and the clerk makes her say all three clauses of her own condition and not have them filled in for her, and she says the first of them to stop agreeing takes it out, that neither has to give a reason to the other first, that the second to find out is the one who acts, and that she is not going to give anybody a reason now and is not going to be asked for one.** **Mero Senn will not put his own fingers in the gap, because he has spent eleven days asking this room to read a wall and not themselves, and refuses to name the man who cut it.** **Renn Vale refuses to ask the man who built it and asks the woman it can hurt, and the answer is two fingers and a habit and thirty-three weeks of her life, and then four people take a piece of beech out of a wall in eleven minutes.** **Bel Ordry's knee goes two-thirds of the way and he finishes the last third on his hands and nobody helps, and he says the knee out loud for the first time in thirty-three weeks and asks for it to be entered separately from this morning, because this morning is a different fact.** The frame goes back to a finger and three-quarters. **Jo Verrick gives the two figures in one breath and says she has no reading from before the second reading and that a number that has only been the same twice in four minutes is not a number anybody should put in a column, and says she is not going to write what caused either of them.** Count one hundred and four.
- **Chapter 170 — A Term This Building Wrote. Fifth day, a Friday, the corridor open, the school ran for the thirtieth morning.** The brace goes back in a foot and a half lower, where the wall is stone and not the repair, **and the carpenter says the ninth stone was never going to be in it, and a beech wedge goes under a hearth stone's bed, which is not a repair and not a brace and not a mark and is the only thing in that anteroom that costs nothing and is honest.** **Five sound wedges have become two, and a door with no wedge in it is a door that has to be watched, and the answer is a person, and the person is the woman who keeps it, and she is one person, and the page where a person would go is still blank, and nobody was sent.** **Talla Roke comes up nine steps and says the thing nobody has said about this building's own terms: a brace has been opened, a brace has been moved, two wedges have gone out of a door, and every office that has ever written a paragraph like that works because nobody can point at the day it stopped being true — and she is not asking this boundary to be the guild, and the difference is not the content, it is that this boundary can be asked in a room by a person who is standing in it.** **The four terms of the fourth asking are read out three times against the original and all three are the same, and they are not struck out because a strike is a lie, and they are not left because a term kept in the letter and broken in the fact and left on the page is a term an office four days up a road can pick up and read as this boundary's practice. The answer is that they bound a work and not a building, and a building outlasts the terms of a work.** The clerk stands at the wall and gives the room away for four minutes and enters that. **The board gains an eighteenth entry: a brace, in the column with nothing over the top of it, because the word over that column would be *keeper* and there is no such word in this building. Tams Quill gives no eleventh reason and says the tenth cost him something he has not worked out, and that a man who reads numbers is not going to guess at one, and that this is the first time in eleven batches that he has had nothing and did not manufacture something, and nobody gives him a reason to have one.** **And the clerk enters, on the last day of a week in which something went right, that this boundary's record says a man in this building recommended shutting a room in the twenty-fourth week, and that it is still true, and that a clerk who enters that only in a bad week is managing a record.** Count one hundred and five. Board walked at eighteen.

## Required character, relationship and power change

- **Power: the Bracer stage is delivered at Chapters 161–170, and it is a stage and not a cure.** The brace is a physical support joined to a bearing mark, **built by a team of five with four hands and one cutter, out of a school's own beech, joined to a mark in anchorstone by fitting the wood to the stone and not the stone to the wood, and no mark was cut.** All four of the bible's requirements are on the page and none is soft: **a tested support** (stone, clay, six beech lengths and two wedges, and the only one of them tested in that room by a man who is paid is the stone); **a counter-bearing** (the party wall, and the low end forbidden, and the reason read out of a clerk's book and not said a second time); **time to let the load settle** (four days in seven, two of them gone, and nobody can beg for it because there is nothing to beg with); **and a mark** (four damaged strokes and one undamaged groove that three people have read three ways and that has still not said anybody its limit). All four costs are on the page: **material, and the material came out of the school**; **a new failure point named in a room before anything was stood under it, and the name is a phrase and not a term**; **people can be hurt while he is protecting them**, and Mero Senn's rule is the instrument and the naming of the person in the brace was a fortnight ago and the naming of the failure point is this batch; **and a coercive brace is brittle, which is a power-system rule and not a metaphor, and every person whose hands are on it agreed out loud in a room to what they are holding and what stops it, and two of them said no and nobody was forced.** **And the brace does not increase the anteroom's capacity. Ten including one litter for no more than two hours, unchanged, said out loud in a room by the woman who cooks, and a brace that makes a room safe by making it hold more people is named as the failure.** **No route change, no binding, no relay, no second bearing mark, no repeatable reading, and no panel of any kind.** The right hand's fine control is not recovered, the left hand does not close, the brass rule is silent for twenty-four weeks, the four marks and the groove answer nothing, and no mark was cut in the stone anywhere in this building.
- **Nera Oduya: the volume's sixth act of trust is spent and is not repeated or inverted. No seventh act is owed and none is taken.** She is **asked for a second work and asks for one**, in the crew's own practice, with the terms out loud, with Renn Vale named as the person who can be told no, and with the three she is not going to ask named with the reason for each before she asks anybody anything. **She is wrong in public, before anybody else finds it, and says so herself, and is not consoled** — and the clerk records that she would have consoled her last week, noticed herself nearly doing it this week, and stopped. **She sets a limit with no number and no day on it: the air in that room stays what it is now, and if it does not, the brace comes out before the air is argued about**, and she says she is not going to be one of the two women who wrote the condition on the brace, because she is not one of the two people it can hurt and will not be able to see what they see. **She says the hard part out loud: she has been the only person who may turn that wheel for thirty-three weeks, she has never once been asked, and she is not going to be grateful about it afterwards, and being right about the air and being the only one who is right is what an authority is, and there is no part of it anybody here should find comfortable.** **Her condition is not met, her second condition stands, nobody asks her in a room, and she does not say her theory.** She is not leaving and no chapter stages her going and the question of her going is not raised. **Their closest moment in this batch is a working moment in front of other people**: the brace across the head of the door, the two inches and a bit that she has been watching for a fortnight, and the fact that she is the person the room believes about the air and Marek Venn is the person the room no longer believes about the wall. **The working consequence of the volume's sixth act, and it is not a scene between them: a man who keeps a page notices out loud that the standing is the first thing in thirty-three weeks that has put itself in a column without anybody's permission, says the half against it, says he is not going to write the line, and the man on the step says out loud that he had it for a week and did not notice it and has been proud of it, and the clerk enters the want and says it does not count for anything.** No intimacy, no recognition scene, no acceleration, and no scene whose only content is the two of them.
- **The working practice holds in every record, and there is not one bare specification block in the batch.** **A fifth asking in a room with the terms out loud and a person who can be told no and the three who could not be asked named with a fourth set of reasons**; **a condition on a brace read out by a second person and compared, in 161 and again in 167 on the day it is met**; **the four terms of the fourth asking read out three times against the original and compared, in 162 and again in 170**; **a survey's four sentences read out by a second person and compared against the book in 164**; **a failure point named by a physician and read back and compared in 166**; **an air work asked in the practice by the person with the authority**; **a school door's condition given in a room by the person who keeps it, with the half against it in the same breath**; **a record of a thing built with a line for a name at the bottom and the name not put there**; **and a term written by a clerk, compared, extended by a room rather than by the clerk, and entered.**
- **Iven Tallo is not redeemed, not a defector, not a martyr, not a saint and not a signatory of anything, and nothing in this batch made him any of those things.** **He is the person who is holding the joinwood while somebody else cuts, and the person whose hand does not close, and the batch did not use that against him and did not use it for him** — the coarse splay is called arithmetic and not a compliment, and the wide face carrying four tons is the carpenter's professional claim and not a consolation. **He is asked to sign the record of the thing he built and says no**, and gives the reason that his name is in the second line of a stiff cover four and a half days up a road and he has not decided about that one, and asks for it to be entered as the reason and not as modesty. **The post is not resolved in this batch in either direction.** He says the brace's failure point is the shim, and that he can say what it is made of and cannot say when it dies, and that anybody who tells you when a thing like that dies is selling you the certainty and not the thing.
- **Bel Ordry: the man with the page is suspicious of the man with the store, out loud, in a room, on a reason, arranged by a clerk, and he is right about the shape of it.** **He says the arrangement was dirty and that he would have said it with the man standing in the room, and he says the half of the suspicion that argues against it, and the rule he states is that a man who has said a thing out loud that argues against his own suspicion is the only kind of suspicion this building has ever had.** He is the fourth of the four hands and what he says stops it is the word, and the half against that is that a word is not a wall. **He raises the objection nobody had raised — that a word can change what it is about — and it is unanswered and the word is said twice anyway.** **He stands in the anteroom and gives the room away for four minutes and enters that she did, and he says his knee out loud for the first time in thirty-three weeks and asks for it to be entered separately from the morning it happened on.** He is still a man and the page is still a page in a coat.
- **Dalla Sen: the clerk is in ten of ten chapters and her work is the batch's spine.** **She asked the crew a fifth time with the terms out loud and named the three who could not be asked with a fourth set of reasons and two of the three are not the names she used last week, and she said the reason for not using the same three is that a person who hears the same name come up five weeks running stops hearing the reason.** **She decided which column the hours went in before she asked anybody, on the same grounds as the twenty-eighth week, and read the terms out three times and added a sixth sentence she had written at the second bell and read as she wrote it.** **She stood at a wall and gave a room away for four minutes and entered that she did.** **She entered the seam and the pot next to each other with nothing between them and refused to write the word brace beside either, and entered that a cook has been holding a pot at nine against the arithmetic of a crew of five, and that she is not going to be the one who says a brace is being paid for in a seam.** **She entered the count at one hundred without a mark beside it and told the room on Thursday so that nobody would be surprised, and said that a clerk who is disappointed that nobody thanks her is a clerk who has begun to want something, and entered that she had noticed and was not going to do anything about it.** **She entered, on the last day of a week in which something went right, that this boundary's record says a man in this building recommended shutting a room in the twenty-fourth week.** **And she did not walk the board in six chapters of the batch and gave a reason in her own hand in each of the six**, and she has now not walked a board for four consecutive batches and the reason in each is that a board is a column with a heading on it and this month was about hands, terms, names, and the day a thing got built.
- **Tams Quill: the crew's column moved by four and a half on a named asking and he took half an hour of it and asked for the split to be named before anybody named it.** **He said no to holding the brace, and gave the reason that the page at the low end has been blank for thirty-two weeks because a person who is willing is not a person who is standing there, and he asked for the reason to be entered before anybody was kind to him about it, and he said he would be good at it.** **He gave the tenth reason for refusing to head the column and it is the second of the ten that is not about him: I do not know what this building is for, and I am not going to print a blank where the answer goes, because a blank under a heading is a hole with a lid on it and a man in a bad month is going to fall into it.** **A week later he entered the eighteenth entry himself, a brace, in the unheaded column, because the word over that column would be *keeper* and there is no such word in this building — and then said he had nothing for an eleventh reason and manufactured nothing, and that the tenth cost him something he has not worked out and that a man who reads numbers is not going to guess at one.** He is still the only person in this building whom the clerk has never once asked.
- **Renn Vale: gave the terms of the asking their hardest clause, and refused to hold the brace, and the reason is the volume's whole argument in one sentence.** **A person who can take a thing out cannot be one of the hands that put it in**, because a room will look for the person whose hand was on it, and if that person is the person allowed to take it out then the room has learned the wrong lesson and it will have learned it from her. **She said the brace's evacuation plan is four rooms and a door and a word and that the last part is not a plan and she is not going to say it again as though it were news.** **She said the half against her own objection on the anteroom's word.** **She refused to ask the man who built it and asked the woman it could hurt instead**, and that is the first time in thirty-three weeks this building has got the right person to ask in the right order. **She said a door with no wedge in it is a door that has to be watched and the answer is one person and the page where a person would go is blank and is not going to be used.**
- **Ilsa Norr: wrote the condition, found out, acted, and made the condition cost something.** **She wrote it a fortnight ago and said she had never liked it and has not come round to it.** **She found out by kneeling at the ninth hearth stone on a shut morning with two fingers and a habit of thirty-three weeks, and she said all three clauses of her own condition and refused to have them filled in for her, and she said she is not going to give anybody a reason and is not going to be asked for one.** **She made the condition cost the one thing it could cost: she said out loud that she is glad the wall read nothing, and that she is one of the two people whose agreement holds the thing up and is therefore the last person in this building who should be glad about a wall, and that she is glad and is not going to stop being glad.** She is the person the brace can hurt, and she is in the room for every part of it, and nobody made her a counter-support and nobody stopped her from being one.
- **Mero Senn: is the instrument and not a rescue, and is right.** **He named the failure point out loud before anything was stood under it, gave it a name that is a phrase and not a term, and refused to give a room a name for a thing he had not looked at.** **He said a wall can stop for a week or stop for good and there is no instrument in this building that tells the two apart.** **He would not put his own fingers in a gap in a floor to find out whether he had been right, and refused to name the man who cut it, because nominating the builder to check the builder is not the same as asking him and he is frightened of his own hands and is not going to do it.** He softens nobody, fixes nothing, and is not a rebuke. **And he is the person who set the limit the brace was measured against: four tons, or thereabouts, with the or thereabouts still in the clerk's book beside it.**
- **Mikka Hale: asked for the fire a seventh, an eighth and a ninth time and refused each time, and asked for the children to be cold in the record in the same place as the refusal.** **She said yes to holding the brace and said the half against it first**, and asked a room to notice that the sentence about not putting a dead man's share into the pot and the sentence about holding a piece of wood for a brace are about the same thing, and that she noticed it first. **She said the anteroom's count out loud, unchanged, in a room, on a morning when a figure on a frame got better for the first time in thirty-three weeks.** She is the fifth of the five that keep this Hearthhold and she is not a lesson.
- **Pell Roke: his work in this batch is reading numbers aloud, and it is all of his work.** **He is named as one of the three who could not be asked, and the reason is that the work is holding and a boy of fourteen on a crutch is not going to be one of the four that hold, and the clerk is not going to be the one who asks him to watch instead.** **He reads nine figures off a slate in 166, having been told what they are first, and says the numbers are a wall and not a count, and reads what the book says and not what the wall says.** He is on the bottom step and comes up as far as the anteroom floor and no further. **He does not go down the steps, into the corridor, or over the waist, in any chapter he appears in, and nobody asked him to. No chapter in this batch puts him in the same room as Talla Roke.**
- **Wen Pask: paid, kept, one hundred and five days and one hundred and five nights into a count at the end of the batch, and dead.** **His count is read at a sixth bell in all ten chapters, is 96, 97, 98, 99, 100, 101, 102, 103, 104 and 105, and is not going to be one hundred and six.** **The count reached one hundred and it was announced on the Thursday before so that nobody would be surprised, and nobody was, and no mark was put beside it, and the clerk entered that a clerk who is disappointed that nobody thanks her is a clerk who has begun to want something.** **It lives in the column with nothing over the top of it and in nothing else, it is not a register, it dates nothing, and it may never be converted into a memorial or stopped.** He is not an argument and not a rescue.
- **Talla Roke: is in the building on her own terms and is not a rescue, and is in two chapters.** **She asks what a person in this building is supposed to do when a piece of beech lets go, and gives the answer: it will have to be a person who is not the man who built it, because if the only person who understands that piece of beech is the man who cut it then in ten years it is a piece of wood and a story, and a story is not a thing somebody can stand next to.** **In Chapter 170 she says the thing nobody has said about this boundary's own terms**: a brace has been opened, a brace has been moved, two wedges have gone out of a door, and every office that has ever written a paragraph like that works because nobody can point at the day it stopped being true. **She says she is not asking this boundary to be the guild and that the difference is not the content, it is that this boundary can be asked in a room by a person who is standing in it.** She says she is not here about a boy in both chapters, and nobody in this batch uses her brother as a route to her.
- **Ormer Hatch: was here and is now gone up the passage, and the suspicion of him is on the page.** **He went up at the first bell on the first day of the thirty-third week with three of his own and the fir from the store, and the beech stays, and the terms stand, and he asked for nothing on his way out.** **A keeper of a page is now suspicious of him out loud, on a reason, arranged by a clerk, and said the half that argues against it.** The store's list has no date on it and can be read on any morning he is asked. He is not an antagonist, not a saint, not a teacher and not a lesson, and the volume's question he asked in the last batch is on the page and unanswered.
- **The woman from the wash rooms: not named, not asked, not rostered, not sent for.** **No legal instrument of any kind was produced in this batch and nothing was struck out, and the count of striking-outs in this book is unchanged at eight.** The floor was not approached, nobody went near it, nobody was sent, and the page at the low end is blank and has been for thirty-two weeks.
- **Oren Sill is not named. Dael Harrow is not named. Cassian Rook does not appear and the One-Seat is not named and Morrowlight is not named. Leena Orr's *a list is not a location* is not used again, and that is now five batches held.** A Quiet Hands cell is uncaught, unrecruited and unread, and **nothing in this batch recruited anybody, turned their reason into a riddle or a voice, or restated it.** No new final enemy.

## Material and labour hand-off

- **The brace is standing.** It is a foot and a half lower than it was on Wednesday, because the bad repair in that wall is at a height and the push came out at the worst of the wall, and the carpenter moved the foot down and put a second length under it. **Its head is a splay cut by a man whose left hand does not close, coarse, and a wider face than a fine cut, and the carpenter says the wide face is carrying four tons and that this is arithmetic and not a compliment.** **Its head bears under the lintel over the school corner's mouth, on a beech shim a finger wide and four inches long in a hollow worn in nine-hundred-year-old stone, and the carpenter says its long edges will crush in about two years, and when they crush the head settles and the frame goes a twelfth of an inch out of true again, and nobody standing in that anteroom is going to feel a twelfth of an inch for a long time, and a twelfth of an inch is the whole of what Chapter 168 measured, so the shim coming out returns the frame to where it was.** **Its joint is tied with the crew's spent nine-metre line, which is spent, and which the carpenter chose over new cord because he would not put a pin in this brace that he would have to argue about in a year, and because a spent line tells you first.** **Its frame reads a finger and two-thirds out of true, down from a finger and three-quarters by a twelfth of an inch, and Chapter 168 is where the first better number in thirty-three weeks was taken and Chapter 170 took the same one twice four minutes apart, and the clerk has entered that a number which has only been the same twice four minutes apart is not a number anybody should put in a column.** **Its failure point is named *the place where it comes out*, in a clerk's book, in a physician's words, and it is a phrase and not a term.**
- **The school door at the far end of nine steps has no wedge in it and is not going to get one back this week.** **Five sound wedges have become two.** Two are in the brace, one is under the ninth hearth stone's bed, and the remaining two are one in the school corner's door and one in the basket. **The answer to a door that has to be watched is a person, the person is the woman who keeps it, she is one person, she keeps a shore, a road, the lead terms, a corridor, a landing and this Hearthhold's second way out of its busiest room, and the page where a person's hours would go is blank and is not going to be used, and nobody was sent anywhere.**
- **The wall at the kitchen-line end.** It has a line of chalk and a piece of string on it at the height of a man's shoulder, which is not a bearing mark and is joined to nothing. **Nine readings have been taken of it at a first bell, eight of them a quarter of an inch and two of them nothing, and the two nothings are the fourth day of the thirty-second week and the first day of the thirty-third, and the fourth day was a Thursday and the first day was a Monday, and the Wednesday between them is a day the school ran and nobody has joined those three facts to each other in a room.** **A bed under the ninth hearth stone is about a third of an inch higher than it was on the Friday morning before the brace went in, and the figure for that came out of two fingers and thirty-three weeks of a habit and not out of a rule, and the clerk entered it and said she is not going to write what caused it.** The plan has no row for that wall, the refusal stands, and the figures are in a book and not on a page.
- **Wedges: five sound and one dead stick, and the dead stick has been missing since the twenty-sixth week.** Two in the brace. One under the ninth hearth stone's bed. One in the school corner's door. One in the basket. **The school door has none.** Nobody touched the third wedge at the waist, which is still up a quarter of an inch and a bit, and the terms of the fifth asking and the fourth both say nobody goes near it and nobody did.
- **The pot: nine days, and it is being held up by a seam.** The seam gave two on Wednesday, one on Friday, one on Tuesday and two on Friday, and **Jo Verrick says four threads and was eight in the nineteenth week and has not measured it in between, and the clerk has said in four weeks running that she is not going to be the one who makes a number for how fast that is going.** **A cook said out loud in a room that five people working for two days on a thing nobody asked them to be paid for in a pot means a pot at nine is going to be eight, and that the only reason it is not eight is a seam, and that she is not going to say a brace is being paid for in that seam, and the clerk entered the two next to each other with nothing between them and did not write the word beside either.**
- **The jars: two a day, entered separately and never added to the pot.** The seam is going quiet and nothing in this batch fixes it.
- **The anteroom's frame: a finger and two-thirds out of true, packed in stone in clay, holding and not fixed, with the mark off the wall and a carpenter in the room who would not put it back.** **Its rating is restored when the word *door* is said in a room and the clerk repeats it, and the count is ten including one litter for no more than two hours, and the brace has not changed either.** **A brace now goes across the head of the door the word is about, and a man who keeps a page has asked in a room whether the word is still about the same thing, and the room has not answered it, and the word was said twice anyway and the objection and the answer are in the same book.** Its second way out is a word, and a carpenter said out loud in this batch that a person does not go out through a word, and nobody answered him, and his question is on the page. **Its fifth term, the air engineer or the elected keeper able to stop it, has been broken since the middle of the twenty-sixth week and the elected keeper is on a road.**
- **The condition on the brace is met and was met, out loud, in a room, by the two people it names, on the second day of the thirty-third week, with a physician's written refusal to certify standing next to it.** The first clause is in force and has been in force since 167. **It is a condition with no number and no day on it, it is in a clerk's book and not on a page, it was met before the volume's climax and before anybody was stood under the brace, and the clerk entered that the finding is not safe and that nobody in that room is entitled to read the word met as the word fine.**
- **The terms of the fifth asking are in the book with six sentences and a split in them: two hours of Iven Tallo's, two of Marek Venn's, half of Tams Quill's.** The six are: the work is not repairing the frame, not fixing the door, not opening anything and not moving anything, and the anteroom's count does not change; the joint is tied and not pegged; the four sentences of the fourth asking stand; **a work that stops is a work that was stopped in a room by a person who said so**; every person whose hands are on the brace agrees out loud to what they are holding and what stops it, and the one who does not agree does not hold; and a man asked for the fifth sentence to be made into a term and then said he did not want it. **The four sentences of the fourth asking were read out three times against the original in Chapter 170 and all three were the same, and they were not struck out and not left, and the finding is that they bound a work and not a building.**
- **Crew hours: seventy-one, up four and a half from sixty-six and a half, on the second day of the thirty-second week**, on a named asking in a room with the terms out loud and Renn Vale named as the person who can be told no, **and the three who could not be asked named with a fourth set of reasons and two of the three are not the names used in the fourth asking: Leena Orr, because she reads things that are already built; Jo Verrick, because the day the brace goes in is the day the wall she reads has a push coming out of it; and Pell Roke, because the work is holding and the clerk is not going to ask him to watch instead.** The crew have now been asked five times in thirty-three weeks and the reasons have not been the same reasons twice.
- **Boundary hours: a hundred and seventeen and a quarter, plus two hundred and twenty-one, = three hundred and thirty-eight and a quarter.** The list adds **by the day the work was done**: **four, two, three, two, three in the thirty-second week; two, three, four, two, three in the thirty-third**, summing to twenty-eight. **Running figures with the day's increment and the total are read out in six chapters — 161, 163, 165, 167, 169 and 170 — and are entered and not read out in 162, 164, 166 and 168, each with a reason in a clerk's hand, and no two of the four reasons is the same reason.** The base is the constant one hundred and seventeen and a quarter, the total is monotonic, both columns reconcile, **and the reconciliation counter is carried at fifteen weeks in the thirty-second week and sixteen in the thirty-third, and is not re-derived inside the batch.**
- **The board: eighteen entries — eleven nulls, one claim, one mark, one row, a plan, a condition, a store and a brace; nine in the ordinary column and nine in the column with the nothing over the top of it.** The claim's condition has not been met. The mark's entry is still on the board. **The eighteenth is a brace, entered by the man who reads the board, in the unheaded column, and the reason is that the word over that column would be *keeper* and there is no such word in this building.** The column is unheaded for the eleventh consecutive batch. **The tenth reason was given in Chapter 164 and the eleventh was not given at all, and that is entered as a change of kind and not as a reason.** The board is walked in four chapters — 161, 164, 167 and 170 — and not in the other six, with a reason in a clerk's hand in each of the six.
- **The instruments.** A carrier's paper with a line at the bottom for a name and no name on it. **Four terms of the fourth asking, read three times and compared, not struck out and not left, entered as having bound a work and not a building.** **A condition met and admitted, and a failure point named as a phrase.** **A sheet of a clerk's own with three findings on it: that the anteroom's word may no longer be about the same thing, that the word was said twice anyway, and that the fourth column stays empty.** **The charter, the plan and the first column are unchanged and none of them left this building in this batch**, and no paper went down the middle passage in this batch except a store's fir going up it on four men's backs and a carrier's stiff cover, which went up in Batch 0001.
- **Unchanged and carried:** the anteroom frame's packing; the second way out of the anteroom as a word, with a carpenter's question about it unanswered; the ninth room, the four treads and the ninth line's *none*; the four households at a second gate; the review page at the low end, blank, with three conditions, the third of them saying that nobody is to be sent anywhere to make it true; the wash-room condition; **the wrapped plate, unopened for thirty-three weeks; the brass rule, silent for twenty-four weeks on the ninth-week origin; Form 9-C; Form 7-B; the bundle in the anteroom; a route-holder's post in a stiff cover four and a half days up a road with a carpenter's name in the second line, neither refused nor accepted; and a person under a lip, packed and not held, with a post that has never been built.**

## Measured craft baseline, for Volume 04 Batch 0003

**Counted on the delivered files of `chapters/volume-04/chapter-0161.md` through `chapter-0170.md` by the method set out below, and validated against `chapters/volume-04/chapter-0151.md` through `chapter-0160.md` as the previous record required and not against Batch 0004's or Batch 0005's.** These are counted, not estimated. **The method did not reproduce Volume 03 Batch 0005's recorded paragraph figures, which is why this record validates against the previous batch's own files, and the validation is recorded below with its result rather than asserted.**

**Narrative share and sentence length, with the method stated.** Quoted words are every word inside a matched pair of quotation marks, as a percentage of the prose words in the file, headers and rules removed and bold markers stripped, per chapter: **66.1, 67.5, 56.9, 69.3, 67.3, 67.1, 63.8, 52.6, 42.1, 57.0, an average of 61.0.** A sentence boundary is taken **inside** each paragraph, after a full stop, a question mark or an exclamation mark followed by whitespace, with quotation marks stripped first, and the split is never run across a whole file. Measured that way on the repaired files: **batch mean sentence 27.5 words, longest 88, and zero sentences of ninety words or more out of 1,265.** **The inherited targets are met without reducing the number of speeches: the chapters whose subject is a set of terms being stated (162, 164, 170) are among the most quoted in the batch at 67.5, 69.3 and 57.0 per cent, and Chapter 169, which is almost entirely one woman saying three clauses of her own condition and a room taking a brace out of a wall, is the least quoted at 42.1.** **No chapter is above seventy.** **The batch mean of 27.5 is under thirty and no sentence is over ninety, and the two figures were reached with staging and shorter speeches, not with fewer speeches.** *Corrected in a review-fix phase: this paragraph first carried 66.0, 67.4, 57.0, 69.2, 67.2, 67.0, 63.7, 52.6, 42.3 and 56.7 at an average of 60.9, and a mean of 28.2 out of 1,234. The longest of 88 and the zero over ninety did reproduce and are kept; the mean, the count and the share series did not reproduce under any method stated in the file, and are replaced above by figures measured under the method this paragraph now states in the same sentence as the figures.*

- **The method.** A paragraph is a block of the raw file split on blank lines. The ten `# Chapter NNN` title lines are not paragraphs and are excluded. A paragraph that begins with a quotation mark is dialogue and is excluded from the paragraph and opener counts. The `---` section rules are counted as non-bold non-dialogue paragraphs, as Batch 0004 set, and no paragraph consisting only of rule characters is dropped from the duplication measure. Bold markers are left in place, so a bolded narrative opener counts as a bold opener. For the duplication measure, **bold section-divider paragraphs are excluded, per Batch 0005's exclusion 3, and the measure is on non-bold content paragraphs only.**
- **Body paragraphs, excluding the chapter headings and the horizontal rules: 702. Paragraphs that do not begin with a quotation mark: 234. Paragraphs that do not begin with a bold marker: 637. Fully-bold paragraphs of any length: 65, of which 34 are under three hundred characters. These four figures are the batch's own and are stated with the exclusions above.**
- **The bold section-divider count is 65 fully-bold paragraphs of any length, of which 34 are under three hundred characters, and that is the definition used.** The figure is reported with the definition and the exclusions in the same sentence, because the previous record established that a divider figure is worthless without both. **The figure inherited from Batch 0001's file — 66 fully-bold, 38 under three hundred — could not be reproduced under any definition that file states, and it was corrected there to 66 and 38 on the delivered files of 151–160. Re-measuring those same delivered files under this batch's method returns 65 and 34, and the difference is two paragraphs, and the finding is that the two methods differ by about three per cent and that the discrepancy was a method difference and not a delivery difference. The rule stands: no batch may carry a divider figure forward without its definition and its exclusions in the same sentence as the figure.**
- **Openers, excluding bolded paragraphs:** *The* 18, *Nobody* 11, *And* 0, *She* 8, *He* 11, **forty-eight together.** Including bolded paragraphs: *The* 29, *Nobody* 13, *And* 2, *She* 8, *He* 11, **sixty-three together.** Batch 0001's including figure was *The* 9, *Nobody* 8, *And* 5, *She* 12, *He* 11. **This batch's *The* figure is 29 against 9, and that is a real change and not a method difference, and the reason is that this batch's chapters are more physical: an anteroom, a trestle, a lintel nine steps up, a shim held up to a lamplight, a knee going two-thirds of the way down a trestle. **The figures were brought down by rewriting twenty-nine openers into other shapes in the writing session, and the batch is within four hundred words of its length before the pass, which is the test that the rewriting was not deletion.** Batch 0003 inherits *The* under twenty.
- **Markup: every one of the ten files is even on asterisks and on quotation marks, every paragraph has an even quotation count and an even bold count, there is no run of three or more asterisks, and there is no doubled quotation mark.** This was measured and **it was wrong three times in the writing session and all three defects were in the files rather than in the reading**: an unclosed speech paragraph in Chapter 166, another in Chapter 168, and a doubled quotation mark in Chapter 167. All three were found by a file-level evenness check and not by reading, **which is the fifth consecutive time in five batches that the check and not the eye has found the defect, and the lesson is now old enough to be a rule: re-measure from disk after any structural edit.**
- **Duplicated non-bold content paragraphs on a ninety-character prefix: three groups, not zero, and both exclusions are now stated in the same sentence as the figure.** The excluded classes are **fully-bold divider paragraphs, which is the sixth-bell house formula, and paragraphs of four words or fewer, which is the interjection class**. The three groups are **"No." in 163 and 165, "Three." in 164 and 167, and "Door," said Dalla Sen. in 167 and 168**; with both exclusions the measure returns zero, **and that zero may not be quoted without both exclusions.** *Corrected in a review-fix phase: this line first read zero on the first exclusion alone, which is false.* Measured across **all twenty files of the volume, 151 through 170**, there are also zero. **Five groups existed at first measure and all five were repaired, and two of them were shared with Batch 0001's closed files and are the finding:** the school-morning paragraph in 153 and 163; the asking's terms in 156 and 166; the condition read out in 159 and 161; the seventeenth-entry read-back in 160 and 161; and the board read in 161 and 167. **Four of the five were the same institutional sentence in two chapters and one was the same read-back, and the batch's own number would have been three and its cross-volume number would have been five, and both were found by running the measure across the whole volume rather than across the batch, which is the rule this record leaves for Batch 0003.**
- **Viewpoint presence: the protagonist is named in ten of ten chapters, with interior life in ten of ten, and this was measured rather than asserted.** He was named and interior on the first measure in seven chapters and at the threshold in three — 163, 167 and 170 — and an interior beat was written into each of the three. **All ten now carry him, and the fifteen passages of physical staging that the measures below record are in his vicinity rather than in anybody else's.**
- **Lengths: 3,143 to 4,103 words, 34,903 in all**, measured as `wc -w` on each file as delivered, headers and rules included. Two chapters are over the soft ceiling of four thousand, **161 at 4,103 and 166 at 4,044**, and they are recorded here rather than quietly delivered. **161 carries the chapter's whole trade argument — a joint, four ways of making two pieces agree, four things this building does not have, a spent line, the condition read out, the problem of time, a standing noticed out loud by a man who keeps a page, and a sixth-bell read — and 166 carries a man going up a passage, a page's suspicion of him, a second asking in full with the three who could not be asked, a failure point named and read back, nine figures read by a boy, the air authority being wrong in public, and a limit set.** The other eight run 3,143 to 3,572. The first pass used the sentence structure as its lever rather than deletion, which is the trade Batch 0001's review-fix pass set. *Corrected in a review-fix phase: this line first carried 3,151 to 4,105 and 34,937, with 161 at 4,105 and 166 at 4,058, and those figures were not reproducible from the files.*
- **Meta language: zero instances of *chapter*, *volume*, *this chapter*, *this volume*, *this batch* or *the reader* in narration**, measured on the ten files. One meta leak was written and cut in the writing session, and **one line was cut from Chapter 162 that said the plan was refused *three volumes ago*, which was a volume reference inside a character's mouth and was replaced with the in-world reference to the plan itself.**
- **People: Talla Roke and Pell Roke are not in the same room in any chapter of the batch, and this was measured and not read.** **She is in 163 and 170. He is in 162, 166 and, unnamed, on a step in 161, 162 and 163.** He is named in 162 as one of the three who could not be asked and in 166 as the boy who read the nine figures, and he is unnamed in 161, 162 and 163 where a boy is on a step.

## Open hand-off

**The Bracer stage is delivered, and the volume's central question is still on the page and is still unanswered.** A brace of a school's own beech is over the head of the anteroom's outer service door, a foot and a half lower than it was on Wednesday, and its shim is in a hollow cut nine hundred years ago and will crush at its edges in about two years, and the term that says so is in a clerk's book in four hands and nobody has been born yet who will read it.

**And the positions the next batch stands on, on the page, with nobody in the room having seen them:** the school door has no wedge and there are two sound wedges left in this building and the answer to a door that has to be watched is one person and the page where a person's hours would go is blank and is not going to be used; **a wall at the kitchen-line end with a chalk line and a string on it and nine readings, eight of them a quarter of an inch and two of them nothing, and a bed under the ninth hearth stone a third of an inch higher than it was on Friday morning, with a figure from two fingers and a habit and not from a rule, and the clerk has said she is not going to write what caused either**; **the pot is at nine days and is being held up by a seam at four threads that was eight in the nineteenth week, and a cook has said the arithmetic out loud and would not say the word**; **four households at a second gate who have nothing**; **a man with a post in a carrier's hand four and a half days up a road who has neither refused it nor accepted it, and a second name refused on a piece of paper in this building because he would not put a name in a second line until he had decided about the first**; **a carpenter whose left hand does not close, who cut a splay, who cut a shim, who said his own joint has a two-year limit and does not know when it dies, and who signed nothing**; **a store four days up the middle passage whose fir went up the road on the Monday, whose beech stays, whose list has no date on it, and which a man who keeps a page is now suspicious of out loud on a reason and a half-reason**; **a schedule with a column headed for the person who will be present, and a third room left standing that nobody in this building knows the name of**; **a board at eighteen entries with nine of them under a column with nothing over the top of it, and a man who reads it who gave a tenth reason this batch, cost himself something doing it, and then said he had nothing and manufactured nothing**; **a condition with no number and no day on it, met, admitted, and in force, with a name in a clerk's book and not on a page**; **a room's rating that is a word said to a page, and an unanswered objection about whether the word is still about the same thing**; **and the question, asked out loud in four words a fortnight ago, who owns a wall. It is on the page, in a clerk's book in the asker's own words, and it was not answered in this batch either, and a courier said in Chapter 170 that the difference between this boundary and a guild notice is that this boundary can be asked in a room by a person who is standing in it.**
