When the clerk asks who releases the crew, **he names himself, out loud, in the room, with the reason**, and the reason is not a number: *a boundary that cannot be left alone is still a rescue.* **Tams Quill's refusal of arithmetic is not withdrawn** and he explains how a refusal and an ending sit on the same page without one of them being a lie. **Nobody leaves and nobody is asked to.** The keeper gives the office's answer to the four houses out loud, in a room, before the first of the month, and it is the true one and it is not good. **And the air engineer asks the volume's question and refuses to answer it**: a halfpenny sheet, a clay packing that shrinks, a word that has to be said in a room and a frame that goes a finger a week is how a door becomes a toll gate, and a page in a coat is **not a system, that is a man**; and she has a theory about why the oldest book in this building has kitchens in it and no treasuries, **it is worse than not knowing, and she will say it when this boundary is keeping itself.** Marek Venn tells the keeper that the eighth line of a column is worth having and that a page in a coat is worth having exactly as much as the man in the coat, and that the road being open is **a fact and not a system.**

## Material and labour handoff

- **The water is found, tested, recorded and in use, and it cost a door, a room's rating, a school day in a worse room, and a woman on a litter going down nine steps.** The seam is a lime-mortar joint with four threads and a leaf-shaped stain **behind the outer service door's frame in the anteroom's haunch**, giving **two jars a day, maybe three**, found by a twenty-two-year-old who counts pails and was nine days late telling anybody.
- **The pot falls twice more and then holds.** Twelve, then **eleven** on the Sunday between the seventeenth and eighteenth weeks, then **ten** on the third day of the eighteenth week, and **ten for the rest of the volume — twelve days without a move, and it has never once gone up.** The seam's water is entered separately in the cook's own numbers and **is never added to the pot**, on Mikka Hale's stated grounds.
- **Pump:** split gland seal, drive-dog key, twenty-six strokes, six minutes off, nineteen. **Five cleanings paid; the estimate stands at ONE**, in Jo Verrick's hand, and she has not written zero. The housing was not opened and the seat was not touched. The pump ran throughout the batch and its window is the only window the anteroom can be emptied in.
- **The anteroom's outer service door** came off its four rusted pins on the second day at the third bell and went back on on the third day at the sixth bell, **packed with stone and clay out of the rubble, holding and not fixed**, and the rating was suspended and restored on a rule: the word *door* is said in a room and the clerk repeats it. **At the fourth day of the nineteenth week a full-room drill found the frame a finger and a bit out of true at the head, because clay shrinks as it dries.** It was shored with stone and lime and a wedge. **It holds and it is not a repair**, and both facts are on the same page and neither cancels the other.
- **Wedges and timber: unchanged.** Seven sound wedges and two dead sticks; **the third wedge at the waist is up a quarter of an inch and a bit**; the ground at the waist has moved three times and is not measured, by three people, on purpose; the newer shore still has no wedge. **Line eleven is still a nothing and the store order that came down with the counter-draft is void.**
- **Crew hours: forty-eight and a half at the end of the seventeenth week, plus four, = fifty-two and a half, given, owed to nobody.** The four are **two on the second day at the door and the haunch behind it**, in the pump's window, asked for by the keeper with the terms out loud; and two on the fourth and fifth days on the fourth column and the counter-draft, in a room. **Every hour of the four is a work, and the record and the three readings are in the boundary's column with the cook's name and the clerk's on them and not in this one, so the crew's hours did not move for the water** — which is Renn Vale's and Sefu Aran's stated principle in Chapter 96 and Tams Quill's in Chapter 100, and all three now say the same thing about the same figure.
- **Boundary hours: a hundred and three and a quarter, plus fourteen, = a hundred and seventeen and a quarter**, and the fourteen-item list adds exactly. **Both columns reconcile exactly.**
- **The column with no heading now holds** a leg, a bundle, **thirty-five days and thirty-five nights**, a half hinge, a line refused, a page that has gone out of sight, and **a wage — the first wage that has ever been in it.** It is **still unheaded**, and Tams Quill has now refused to head it in three consecutive batches, and the reason given this batch is that a heading turns a wage into something a bad month can be pointed at.

## Character and labour changes

- **Marek Venn** read a page and got a room, and it cost him his left hand for a night and his judgement for an hour, and he said out loud that it did not save anybody and was worth an afternoon, and that his left hand closed properly that morning and does not close tonight. **He refused to sign the charter in nine seconds** because a signature by a man who is leaving is a name against a route. He was named in **seven of the ten chapters** and is in both of the last two.
- **Nera Oduya** was asked for a piece of work instead of consulted for a decision, arranged four hours of the boundary running without her and entered the arrangement and its price, refused to sign the charter because the air engineer does not sign anything in this Hearthhold, **struck the woman's covers out of a legal document because nobody had asked her**, put three *I will not*s on a page, put the air board on the page instead of in a coat, left the thirteenth line unwritten with the reason on the page, was wrong about the direction of the school corner's air for seventeen weeks and said so, and **asked the volume's question out loud and refused to answer it.**
- **Renn Vale** refused to hold the outer service door shut and gave the price of that refusal, entered the suspension and the word-*door* rule, wrote the reason the fourth column cannot be headed and had the modal struck out of it, entered the price of putting the stair inside a work two batches ago and has not withdrawn it, and said *a good week is not a work.*
- **Dalla Sen** asked *what is it that is a room*, read the record out and repeated the correction and then repeated the strike, **refused the keeper the chalk in front of the room**, wrote nothing in the dispute between two men about the order of a fortnight, asked in front of the room who releases the crew so that the ending would not be a number, and entered the bell observation at the sixth bell.
- **Bel Ordry** ruled that a keeper of a page is not a holder of a route, refused Talla Roke's sheet in his own words, took the third column on a rule about a word and put the rule in the third column, asked for the chalk and was refused it, **wrote no name at the top of the charter, signed a charter with no name on it, refused to sign it with anything else**, and was told to his face by Marek Venn that a page in a coat is worth having exactly as much as the man in the coat.
- **Tams Quill** put the tenth null in the ordinary column and did not move it, put **the first wage in the column with no heading and did not head the column**, entered the Reader claim as a claim and refused to call it a reading in a room where a plan is going to a stranger, read both columns having named no day, and did not end an obligation on a figure when he was asked by the keeper not to.
- **Sefu Aran** rewrote clause two of the charter against himself, signed first, **refused to use his own condition**, named himself as the person who discharges the obligation, gave the answer to the four houses out loud, and said that a boundary which cannot be left alone is still a rescue.
- **Iven Tallo** refused the guild's draft and would not explain why in the room, said the real reason afterwards on a bench to one man, refused to sign the boundary's own charter, and **told the boy that he will be paid and will not be a carpenter and that being kept is not being trained.** He is not a defector, not a martyr, not a saint, and not redeemed.
- **Wen Pask** found the water, told the room nine days late and was not thanked and not sent anywhere, asked out loud whether a courier route carries people and was answered in front of the room by a sheet, and is paid a wage that lives in a column with nothing over the top of it and is thirty-five days and thirty-five nights into a count.
- **Mikka Hale** struck the word *clean* out of a water record and wrote the true one in her own hand, refused to add two sources into one number, took the second gate out of a charter, and made the keeper explain why his page has no name on it. **She writes no line of the third column in this batch** — the four lines in Chapter 94 are Jo Verrick's, Renn Vale's, Ilsa Norr's and Nera Oduya's, and her only hand on the page is the strike.
- **Jo Verrick** named the haunch she had touched every day for fifteen weeks, did not start anything on a packed frame, found the finger with a folding rule on her own knee, shored it, and said twice that it holds and is not fixed.
- **Ilsa Norr** made the school run in a worse room and would not have it written as *moved*, and it was struck and entered in her own words.
- **Harlan Merrow** arrived, was honest for two days, read the guild's case including the head he did not write, conceded all three objections and then made the best argument against them, said the office will put the draft to four other Hearthholds and that three will accept and three will be cited against this one, and said he will recommend the charter and does not expect it to be granted. **The Cartwright Guilds are not defeated and are not made wicked.**
- **Nell Ferrin** put the true sentence on the back of a halfpenny advertisement and would not pretend she thinks it will fail.
- **Talla Roke** is in this batch in a courier's hand **and is not in the building.** She has started a courier route outside the guild system with three named people of her own, she is not asking this boundary for anything, **she is not a rescue, she is not offering the boy anything, and the spring is not owed by anybody and is not paid off as a gift.**
- **The woman from the wash rooms** is not named, is not asked, and is not on a page as a name anywhere in this batch. **Her three conditions stand unsoftened, the floor was not approached, and the charter does not mention her covers — because the air engineer struck them out of it before anybody had asked her.**
- **Oren Sill is not named in this batch.** The column with no heading still holds a line refused in the fourteenth week and it has not been filled by a feeling.

## Open handoff

**Volume 02 is closed.** The charter is signed, the road up the middle passage is open and cannot be shut for a delivery and nobody may be charged for it, the plan is in a coat with **eight of the nine lines' third column filled and one empty, which the keeper will not name** and a fourth column that is empty with a reason on it in two hands, and a man the room has ruled is not a holder of a route is the man who holds it.

Still open, named, and deliberately unresolved: **the four households at the second gate**, who still have nothing and who the keeper has promised to go and tell so in person before the first of the month; **the frame that goes a finger and a bit and is holding and is not fixed**; **the school corner's mouth**, shut again, warm, and going the wrong way; **the air valve, asked and not answered, and the spring**; **the name Nera Oduya said in her own hand in the twelfth week, still not asked, still not to be asked by a room**; **the theory she has held since the fourth day of the eleventh week about why the oldest book in this building has kitchens in it and no treasuries**; **the ninth line and the four treads and the eighth term**; **the two sentences on the blank line, and the row with an empty third column that is empty by a woman's own condition**; **the seat**, with one cleaning in it and the hunt closed; **the waist**, moved three times, wedge up a quarter and a bit, no measurement; **the bar**, seven mornings, six of nothing after three; **Wen Pask, paid, kept, thirty-five days, not a carpenter**; **the word *maintainer*** in a clerk's hand; **the guild's fourth paragraph**, refused three times; **Iven Tallo's bond**, and an apprentice off the roll, and a refusal on the ground that he does not know what he would say in the spring; **the wrapped plate**, unopened, unidentified, joined to nothing; **the brass rule**, silent for ten weeks and put on a table once and looked at for a second by nobody; **Form 9-C**, unattended; **Form 7-B**, unsigned; **the halfpenny sheet and the three Hearthholds that will have timber in the month after next**; and **a person under a lip.**

## Measured craft baseline, for Volume 03's first batch

Counted on the delivered files by the same method that reproduces Batch 0004's recorded figures — **paragraphs that do not begin with a quotation mark**, on a 90-character prefix for duplication. **These figures were recounted after the review repairs of this batch and are the counted values, not estimates.**

- ***read back* and *read it back*: 3**, against **3** in Batch 0004, 4 in Batch 0003, 7 in Batch 0002 and 32 in Batch 0001. Held, and the phrase was cut from ten places to make room for it. **Counted separately, *read it out* appears 11 times** and is not part of this class; the two must not be added together, and an earlier draft of this line conflated them.
- **Paragraph openings beginning *The …*: 6**, against **16** in Batch 0004, 17 in Batch 0003, 33 in Batch 0002 and 46 in Batch 0001. The downward trend continues and the class is now the smallest in the batch.
- **Paragraph openings beginning *Nobody …*: 8**, against **11** in Batch 0004, 13 in Batch 0003 and 9 in Batch 0002. **Held at or under eleven.**
- **Paragraph openings beginning *And …*: 2**, against **12** in Batch 0004. **The device is retained and not used; two is under the target of eight.** The next batch should hold it under eight and should not let it fall to zero, because the beat is doing work.
- **Paragraph openings beginning *She …* (2) or *He …* (9): 11 across the batch**, against 21 in Batch 0004 and 22 in Batch 0003. Held well under thirty. **The *She* figure is low and the next batch should watch it: this batch is carried by documents and by men at documents, and a batch with a woman's interior at the front of it will move it.**
- **Duplicated paragraphs on a 90-character prefix: zero.**
- **Bare specification blocks: zero.** The water record is read out, objected to in two specific lines and corrected in three hands; the third column is filled line by line with a person standing for each line; the fourth column's reason is written in Renn Vale's hand and the modal is struck in Nera Oduya's; the guild's six-head draft is read out and the writer names the head he did not write; the charter's eight clauses are objected to out loud before anybody picks up the chalk and five of them are rewritten in the objector's hand; the board is walked twice with a fourteen-item list; and the Reviewer's claim is entered as a claim with a condition. **There is not one specification block in the batch.**
- **Chapter lengths 2,345 to 3,985, 27,760 in all.** **Every chapter is under 4,000 words and the longest is 3,985.** The regression named in Batch 0004's own summary is not repeated. **The batch total is roughly 8,500 words below Batch 0004's and this is deliberate: the volume's last ten chapters are a set of arguments about documents, and the padding that made the earlier batches longer was repetition of the device, not length of scene.**
- **Eight of ten chapters are staged somewhere other than the anteroom bench**: 91 is a kitchen and a doorframe, 92 is a corridor and nine steps, 93 is a bench in a kitchen and a corridor and a shut mouth, 94 is a table and a landing, 95 is a room with a flat case open on it, 96 is a kitchen, 97 is the anteroom at a table with a charter on it, 98 is the clinic return and the anteroom at its number, 99 is a drill and a shoring, 100 is a room and a stair head.
- **Marek Venn is named in eight of the ten chapters** (91, 92, 94, 95, 97, 98, 99 and 100, with interior life in all eight). The floor of six is passed. **He is in both of the last two chapters**, which the previous batch did not achieve, and his closing intervention is the volume's last character beat before the next volume's question. **It has two halves and both are load-bearing: he tells the keeper to his face that the open road is not worth a line, because a road is a road and this building did not build it and nobody can point at the thing that did — and then he refuses to let that answer run together with the eighth line, which he says is worth having, and which is a page in a coat worth having exactly as much as the man in the coat.** A reader who takes only the first half has misread the volume's argument, and the state file must not report only the second half.
- **No forward count of days or weeks is put on a courier anywhere in the batch.** Talla Roke's sheet is dated by nothing, Harlan Merrow's road is a road, and the halfpenny sheet arrives without anybody saying when it was printed. **The batch's discovery about a notice with no date on it is not repeated and is not corrected**; the discovery stays *out of our sight*.

## Calendar, fixed and carried forward

**The week runs Monday to Sunday. Chapters 91–95 are the eighteenth week** — 91 the first day, a Monday, the corridor shut; 92 the second day, a Tuesday, the door off; 93 the third day, a Wednesday, the corridor open and the school run in a worse room; 94 the fourth day, a Thursday, the third column and the fourth column's reason; 95 the fifth day, a Friday, the counter-draft. **Chapters 96–100 are the nineteenth week** — 96 the first day, a Monday, Talla's notice and the wage; 97 the second day, a Tuesday, the charter; 98 the third day, a Wednesday, the corridor open and the school running, the hand-over; 99 the fourth day, a Thursday, the corridor shut and the drill; 100 the fifth day, a Friday, the corridor open and the volume close.

**The standing arrangement holds for both weeks: the corridor is shut on the first, second and fourth days, open on the third, fifth and sixth, and the seventh day is not a crossing day. The school runs on the three open days.** The corridor is shut on **nine of the ten days this batch**, and **the school ran in a worse room on one of its three days and there was no lost week and nobody was told a day had been paid back.** **A later batch may not restore a school day without restoring a closure, and may not tell Ilsa Norr which of the two is independent.**

**The spoken measure of elapsed weeks is a separate register and it was held where Batch 0004 left it.** Tams Quill says *fifteen weeks* in Chapter 94 and is not corrected; other voices say *eighteen* and *nineteen* for the same building, which is the drift Batch 0004 already had and which is **not** an inconsistency in the book but a property of the register. **A later batch may not "correct" it, and may not use a spoken count to date an event.**

**Wen Pask's count runs one a day from the locked base of twenty-three on the fifth day of the seventeenth week: twenty-six on the first day of the eighteenth, twenty-seven on the second, thirty on the fifth, thirty-one on the first day of the nineteenth, thirty-two on the second, thirty-four on the fourth, and thirty-five on the fifth day of the nineteenth.** It is in the column with no heading and in nothing else, and it is **thirty-five days and thirty-five nights** at the volume's close. **It is his own count, kept by a clerk, and it is not a register, and it must not be used to date anything.**

**No day number is attached to any in-batch event, and the highest value in the day-number register remains the forty-sixth day.** The only date on any object remains the date in the top corner of the plan, and it is still the plan's whole distinction from the dowel.

## Relationship, thematic, and power change

**The Reader stage is granted here, once, and it is a stage and not a solution.** It identifies **a likely failure in one room** — the anteroom, and then a **fingerprint of stone** — with **fatigue and sensory bleed**: a headache behind the left eye with a floor to it, a left hand that clamped and shook and did not close that night, the count of a room arriving as a sound under the pump's check, and the count still audible in a room he was not in. **The cost is not erased by anything in this batch**: the shoring is done by two other people, the frame is still out of true, the left hand is still not right, and the man himself says that it did not save anybody and was worth an afternoon. **The risk of treating a first impression as fact is the scene's centre**: he says it as a fact before he has decided anything, an air engineer makes him say how he knows, and the count enters it as **a claim, not a reading**, with a condition. **There is no bearing mark, no route change, no binding, no relay, and no brace**, and Marek Venn refuses in advance to read a boundary. **His permanent fine-control loss in the right hand is not recovered.** The brass survey rule is taken out of the coat's pocket by the clerk, because his right hand could not open it, and it lies on a table saying nothing for the tenth week, and nobody looks at it.

**Nera is a collaborator and not a dependent, and the change is legible in three scenes rather than in one.** She is asked for a piece of **work** and not consulted for a decision. **The room does without her for four hours in front of the reader and says so in front of the reader**, and she entered the arrangement and states its price, which is that a room which can do without the person stops asking, and that this is the price and she paid it and is not going to be kept another four months by a boundary that needs her. She is **wrong** — about the direction of the school corner's air, and about the anteroom's danger, which is the word and not the stone — and she is wrong in public and is not consoled. She **refuses to sign the charter** and gives a structural reason and nobody asks her to. She **strikes the woman's covers out of a legal document** because nobody had asked the woman. She **refuses to hand the air board to a man** and puts it on a page instead. And at the volume's close she is the person who asks the next volume's question out loud and **refuses to answer it**, which is the last exercise of an authority nobody delegated to her.

**Talla begins organising a courier route outside the guild system, and it is hers.** It is a sheet in her own hand with three other names under hers, addressed to nobody in this building, saying that a paper with their mark is theirs and a paper without it came from a counter. **The boundary's contribution is a sentence, and then two, and neither is a page and neither is signed by anybody.** **She is not a rescue, she is not in the building, she is not offering the boy anything, and the spring is not owed by anybody and is not paid off as a gift** — it is named at the volume's close as owed by nobody and asked for by nobody, and the charter's road is not a spring.

**The volume's question is now two questions and one refusal.** *Why do the Lattice's rooms remember domestic routines* has been held by the air engineer since the fourth day of the eleventh week, is worse than not knowing, and is **said aloud in a room in Chapter 100 and not answered**, on the condition *when this boundary is keeping itself.* *What happens when a door becomes a toll gate* is answered for this boundary in a charter and answered for the world by nothing: a halfpenny sheet, three Hearthholds that will have timber, a frame that goes a finger a week, and a page in a coat. **Marek Venn's last act in Volume 02 has two halves and both are the argument.** He tells the keeper that **the open road is not worth a line** — a road is a road, it will be a road in a bad month too, this building did not build it, and nobody can point at the thing that did — and then he refuses to let that answer run together with the eighth line, which **is** worth having, and which is a page in a coat worth having exactly as much as the man in the coat. **He also says out loud, in the same breath, that the road being open is a fact and not a system** — which is the series' central flaw, named by its owner, one volume early and in a room.

**The stair is still not a holder and the stair is not in any work and the plan is not a route, and nobody goes down the four treads and the eighth term stands.** The anteroom's row has a third column and does not have the word *person* in it, and the ninth room has no row and a charter clause that says so on purpose, and both women who refused it still refuse it.

## Review and repairs

**An independent review of this batch was run and the log is `logs/batch-0005.review.log`.** Its structural commitments were found delivered and bounded and its guardrails all held. **The findings were repaired in the chapters and in this state, not recorded and deferred**, and the repairs that changed a fact are:

1. **The crew's figure contradicted itself inside the volume's own "not on a figure" beat — the highest-value repair.** Chapter 100 listed *two on the second day at the haunch and the record and the water* among the four crew hours and then said, two clauses later, that the water cost the crew nothing, and three people had already said the same in Chapter 96. **The two second-day hours are the door and the haunch behind it and every one of the four is a work; the water's record and its three readings are in the boundary's column with the cook's name and the clerk's on them.** Chapter 100 now says so inside the count's own speech.
2. **The crew figure's own timeline did not run.** Forty-eight and a half was asserted on the fourth and fifth days of the eighteenth week and on the first day of the nineteenth, after two hours had been added on the second day of that week. **The figure is now fifty and a half from the second day, fifty-one and a half on the fourth, and fifty-two and a half from the fifth day of the eighteenth week onward**, and the clerk states her own wrong prediction out loud in Chapter 94 rather than being corrected in it. **Fifty-two and a half at the volume's close, as recorded everywhere else, is unchanged.**
3. **Wen Pask's count had drifted two days high from the first day of the nineteenth week.** The locked base is twenty-three on the fifth day of the seventeenth week and the count runs one a day. **It is thirty-one on the first day of the nineteenth, thirty-two on the second, thirty-four on the fourth, and thirty-five at the close**, and his "I have been in it four days" in Chapter 95 is now thirty days, which is the eighteenth-week figure the state files had recorded but the prose had not carried.
4. **The third column's fill count was half-repaired.** "Six empty lines" survived in Chapters 94, 95 and 98 after Chapter 94 filled four of them, and "the sixth line" survived in the same chapter that calls it the eighth. **Chapter 91's six is correct and stands — it is the count before the filling — and every later mention is now two, or one after Chapter 98.**
5. **Chapter 100's closing exchange read as a contradiction.** Marek Venn opened "It is not worth it" and praised the eighth line three clauses later. **He now names the referent: the road is not worth a line, the eighth line is a different object, and he will not have the two run together in a room, because that is how a good answer gets used to pay for a bad one.**
6. **Chapter 98 put the air engineer in two places in one bell.** She is at the clinic return from the first bell for four hours and was asked in a room at the fourth bell in the same breath. **The asking is at the fifth bell, after the six things are read and after she is back in the room.**
7. **This state file contradicted the prose twice** — it credited Mikka Hale with the third column's fourth line, which is Nera Oduya's and which Mikka does not appear in Chapter 94 to write, and it said the plan had six of nine third-column lines filled when eight are. **Both corrected, and Mikka's only hand on the page in this batch is her strike of the word *clean*.**
8. **The measured craft baseline did not reproduce.** The opener counts and the batch word total were estimates that did not match the files. **They have been recounted from the delivered chapters and this section now carries the counted values and states its method.**
9. **Dispatch was blocked.** `workspace/volume-02/batch-0005/.done` was never written, so both dispatchers re-selected the completed batch and Volume 03 was unreachable. **The marker is written and exactly one next-phase prompt exists, `workspace/phase-003-volume-03-outline/PROMPT.md`.**
10. **A stray figure in this file.** The Chapter 91 entry said the boundary was *thirty-three days short of water*, which is not the pot count and is a leftover of the broken Wen Pask figure. **It is twelve days, which is the pot at the end of the seventeenth week and what Chapter 91 opens on.**

**One finding was inspected and deliberately not actioned.** `workspace/volume-02/batch-0004/.wip-conflict` is a controller-written marker from a previous run. It is inert, because that phase has a `.done` and the runner only consults the marker for a phase it is about to re-run. **It is a controller artifact and is left in place.**

**One finding is flagged and not touched, because it is controller-owned.** `state/phase-ledger.json` still reads `currentPhase: volume-01/batch-0002` at a hundred delivered chapters, and its phase list stops at that batch. **The ledger is written by the workflow, not by the writer or the fixer, and was not edited.**
